continuous mapping (see
[
Tri95
, Thm. 2.9.3]
), and we obtain from
(
3.29
)

\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\left|k\right|^{3}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right).

(3.30)

This allows us to estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{defutilde})}}{\leq}C\left(\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{Rcurlcurlu0}),
(\ref{eq:utilde-20})}}{\leq}C\left(\left|k\right|^{3}\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right).

(3.31)

To estimate
\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}\left(\Omega\right)}
we use the fact (see
[
Tri95
]
) that
the Besov space is an interpolation space
B_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)=\left(L^{2}(\Omega),H^{1}(\Omega)\right)_{1/2,1}
(via the so-called real method of interpolation). For
t\in(0,1]

select
(\mathbf{r}_{0})_{t}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\Omega)
as given by
Lemma
3.9

and estimate with the interpolation inequality (by using the notation as in
Lemma
3.9
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}-\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}+\left\|\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}-\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|^{1/2}\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}-\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)}^{1/2}+\left\|\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Lem.~\ref{lemma:interpolation-inequality}}}{\leq}C\left(t^{1/4}\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{1/2}+t^{-1/4}\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}^{1/2}\right)+\left\|\left(\mathbf{r}_{0}\right)_{t}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{Lemintineqa}), (\ref{Lemintineqb})}}{\leq}C\left(\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}+t^{1/2}\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+\sqrt{1+|\ln t|}\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(t^{1/2}\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+\sqrt{1+\left|\ln t\right|}\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\left(\Omega\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{Rcurlcurlu0}), (\ref{H1estr0u0})}}{\leq}C\left(t^{1/2}\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|+\sqrt{1+\left|\ln t\right|}\left|k\right|^{2}\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Omega)}\right).

Using (
3.16
) we get

\displaystyle\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\leq C\left(\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|^{2}+\left|\left(\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\right)^{2}-\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|^{2}\right|\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|k\right|^{-1/2}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\right)\overset{\text{(\ref{curlu0est1/2})}}{\leq}C\left(\left|k\right|^{3}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|\mathbf{r}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{B}_{2,1}^{1/2}\left(\Omega\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|k\right|^{3}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+t^{1/2}\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|+\sqrt{1+\left|\ln t\right|}\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\left(\Omega\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{u0est1/2})}}{\leq}C\left(\sqrt{1+\left|\ln t\right|}\left|k\right|^{3}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+t^{1/2}\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\right).

Selecting
t\sim 1/\left|k\right|^{2}
sufficiently small implies

\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\leq C\left|k\right|^{3}\sqrt{1+\ln\left|k\right|}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

We conclude from this and (
3.31
)

\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}\left(\Omega\right)}+\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right\|\leq C\left|k\right|^{3}\sqrt{1+\ln\left|k\right|}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

(3.32)

Combining (
3.32
) and (
3.15
) yields

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{3}\sqrt{1+\ln\left|k\right|}\left\|\mathbf{\tilde{g}}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:stability-estimate-gtilde})}{\leq}C\left|k\right|^{3}\sqrt{1+\ln\left|k\right|}\left(\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+|k|^{1/2}\|\mathbf{j}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right).

(3.33)

Step 7.
Combining (
3.9
),
(
3.13
), and (
3.33
), we
have arrived at

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g}_{T})\right\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq\|k^{-2}\nabla\psi\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}+\|\mathbf{u}_{\mathbf{j}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}+\|\mathbf{u}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{3}\sqrt{1+\ln\left|k\right|}\left(\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+|k|^{1/2}\|\mathbf{j}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\right),

which is the claimed estimate.

Lemma 3.9
(
[
MR21
, Prop. 4.14]
)

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a
bounded Lipschitz domain. Then there is
C>0
such that for every
w\in H^{1/2}(\Omega)
and every
t\in\left(0,1\right]
there exists some

w_{t}\in H^{1}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\left\|w-w_{t}\right\|+t\left\|w_{t}\right\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq Ct^{1/2}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{1/2}\left(\Omega\right)},

(3.34)

\displaystyle\|w_{t}\|_{B_{2,1}^{1/2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{1+\left|\ln t\right|}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{1/2}(\Omega)}.

(3.35)

4
Maxwell’s equations with the “good” sign

4.1
Norms

We consider Maxwell’s equations with the “good” sign and first describe the spaces for the given
data.
Since the sesquilinear form
A_{k}\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)
is considered
in the space
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
, the natural space for the
right-hand side is its dual
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}
. In
our setting, the right-hand side is given in
\Omega
via the volume data

\mathbf{j}
and on
\Gamma
via the boundary data
\mathbf{g}_{T}
.
In order to view
\mathbf{j}
and
\mathbf{g}_{T}
as elements of

\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}
, we introduce the spaces

\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)

and
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
.
By using the usual notation
V^{\prime}
for the dual space of a normed vector
space
V
we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp},0}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}:\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{w}=0\right\}={\{\nabla\varphi\,|\,\varphi\in H_{\operatorname{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)\}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)\right)^{\prime}\cap\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp},0}^{\prime},

(4.1)

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1}(\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma},\Gamma)

\displaystyle:=\left\{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1}(\Gamma)\mid\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1}(\Gamma)\right\},

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)\cap\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1}\left(\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma},\Gamma\right).

(4.2)

and equip the spaces
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)

and
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
with the norms
(cf. also Lemma
4.1
below)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{|({\mathbf{f}},{\mathbf{v}})|}{\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}},

(4.3)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),k}

\displaystyle:=\sup_{{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}}\frac{|({\mathbf{g}}_{T},{\mathbf{v}}_{T})_{{\mathbf{L}}^{2}(\Gamma)}|}{\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}}.

(4.4)

We also introduce for
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1/2}\left(\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma},\Gamma\right)
(cf.
(
2.17
))

\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}:=|k|\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}+\left|k\right|^{2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}\left(\Gamma\right),k}.

(4.5)

An equivalent norm that is more naturally associated with the intersection
spaces
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)
and
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1

The spaces
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)
and
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
can
be viewed in a canonical way as subspaces of
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}
, and there holds the norm equivalences

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}\left(\Omega\right),k}

\displaystyle\sim\!\!\sup_{\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}\left(\Omega\right):\nabla\varphi\neq\mathbf{0}}\frac{|(\mathbf{f},\nabla\varphi)|}{\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}}+\sup_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}}\frac{|(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{z})|}{\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{z}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}},

(4.6)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}\left(\Gamma\right),k}

\displaystyle\sim\sup_{\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}\left(\Omega\right):\nabla\varphi\neq\mathbf{0}}\frac{|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|}{\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}}+\sup_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}}\frac{|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\mathbf{z}_{T})_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|}{\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{z}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}}

(4.7)

with constants implied in
\sim
that are independent of
\left|k\right|\geq k_{0}
.

Proof.

Proof of (
4.6
):
Since
\nabla\varphi\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
for
\varphi\in H_{\operatorname{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
and
{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\Omega)\subset{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}
, the right-hand side of
(
4.6
) is easily bounded by the left-hand side.
For the reverse estimate, we decompose any element
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
with the aid of
Lemma
2.7
as
\mathbf{v}=\nabla\varphi+\mathbf{z}
with
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
and
\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}+|k|\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|k|^{1/2}(\|\mathbf{v}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)})+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k},

(4.8)

where, in the last step we used the multiplicative trace estimate

\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}\leq C\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}
. This implies that the left-hand side of
(
4.6
) can be bounded by the right-hand side.

Proof of (
4.7
):
The proof is analogous
to that of (
4.6
).

Note that
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\subset\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)
and
{\mathbf{L}}_{T}^{2}(\Gamma)\subset\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
with continuous embeddings as can be
seen from the following reasoning. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
or
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)
or

\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)
and for

\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{L}_{T}^{2}(\Gamma)
or
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)
or
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}(\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma},\Gamma)
, we have by direct
estimations

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}\!\leq\!C|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\!\leq\!C|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}\!\leq\!C|k|^{-2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k},\!\!

(4.9)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),k}\leq C|k|^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma),k}

\displaystyle\phantom{\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),k}}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\leq C|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma),k}.

(4.11)

We also have the following result for
\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}
:

Lemma 4.2

There is
C>0
depending only on
\Omega
such that

\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\leq C\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma),k}+|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma),k}.

Proof.
We use the minimum norm lifting
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}
from
(
6.13
) with the property that
\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\geq\|\nabla\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}(\varphi|_{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
for arbitrary
\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
. By continuity of the trace mapping, we
get
\inf_{c\in\mathbb{R}}\|\varphi-c\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\nabla\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}(\varphi|_{\Gamma})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
. An integration by parts shows for arbitrary
\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
and arbitrary
c\in\mathbb{R}

|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|=|(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T},\varphi-c)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|\leq\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\|\varphi-c\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}.

Taking the infimum over all
c\in\mathbb{R}
yields, for arbitrary
\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
,

|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|\leq C\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

and we conclude

\sup_{\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega):\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi\neq\mathbf{0}}\frac{|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|}{|k|^{1/2}\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+|k|\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}}\leq C|k|^{-1}\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}.

Similarly, for
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)
we estimate

|(\mathbf{g}_{T},\mathbf{z}_{T})_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}|\leq C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}
. Hence,

\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}\left(\Gamma\right),k}\leq C\left(\left|k\right|^{-1}\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\right).

(4.12)

The result follows.

4.2
The Maxwell problem with the good sign

The Maxwell problem with the good sign reads:
Given
\displaystyle\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)
and
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),
find
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
such that

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,\operatorname*{i}k}\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{f}\text{ in }\Omega\quad\text{and\quad}\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{g}_{T}\text{ on }\Gamma.

(4.13)

The weak formulation is:

\text{find }\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}\quad\text{s.t.\quad}A_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{v})=\left(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{v}\right)+\left(\mathbf{g}_{T},\mathbf{v}_{T}\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}\quad\forall\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}},

(4.14)

where the sesquilinear form
A_{k}^{+}
is given by

A_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}):=\left(\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u,}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{v}\right)+k^{2}\left(\mathbf{u,v}\right)-\operatorname*{i}k\left(\mathbf{u}_{T},\mathbf{v}_{T}\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}.

(4.15)

The solution operator is denoted
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})\mapsto\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T}
). In this section, we
develop the regularity theory for problem (
4.13
). Indeed, as the
following Theorem
4.3
shows,
(
4.14
) is uniquely solvable.

Theorem 4.3

Let
\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz
domain with simply connected boundary. Then there is
C>0
independent of
k

such that the following holds:

(i)

The sesquilinear form

A_{k}^{+}
satisfies
\operatorname{Re}A_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{v},\sigma\mathbf{v})=2^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}_{\operatorname{imp},k}
for all

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
, where
\sigma=\exp\left(\frac{\pi\operatorname*{i}}{4}\operatorname*{sign}k\right)
.

(ii)

The sesquilinear form is
continuous:
|A_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})|\leq\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}
for all

\mathbf{u}
,
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
.

(iii)

The solution
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
of (
4.13
)
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|k|^{-1}\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left|k\right|^{-1/2}\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\right),

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}+\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),k}\right),

(4.17)

provided
\left(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T}\right)\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{L}_{T}^{2}(\Gamma)
for
(
4.16
) and
\left(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T}\right)\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma)
for (
4.17
).

(iv)

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. If
\Gamma
is sufficiently smooth and
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)
,
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\Omega\right),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{-3}\left(\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right),

(4.18a)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+|k|\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}\left(\Gamma\right),k}\right).

(4.18b)

Proof.

Proof of (
i
),
(
ii
):
For
(
i
) we compute

\operatorname{Re}\left(A_{k}^{+}(\mathbf{v},\sigma\mathbf{v})\right)=\operatorname{Re}\left(\bar{\sigma}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}^{2}+\operatorname*{i}\bar{\sigma}k\left\|\mathbf{v}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}^{2}\right)=\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}^{2}.

The continuity assertion (
ii
) follows
by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Proof of (
iii
):
The estimate
(
iii
) follows directly from a variant of
the Lax-Milgram lemma: We choose
\mathbf{v}=\mathbf{u}
in the weak form
(
4.14
) and estimate

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}^{2}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}A^{+}(\mathbf{u},\sigma\mathbf{u})=\operatorname{Re}\left(\left(\mathbf{f},\sigma\mathbf{u}\right)+\left(\mathbf{g}_{T},\sigma\mathbf{u}_{T}\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\prime}(\Gamma),k}\right)\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

(4.19)

from which (
4.17
) follows. Estimate
(
4.16
) is then obtained from
(
4.17
) and (
4.9
),
(
4.10
).

Proof of (
iv
):
From now
on, we assume that
\Gamma
is sufficiently smooth. We proceed by induction on

m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and show that if the solution
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)
, then
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)
. Specifically, after the
preparatory Step 1, we will show
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
in
Step 2 and
\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
in
Step 3. Step 4 shows the induction hypothesis for
m=0
including the norm
bounds. Step 5 completes the induction argument for the norm bounds.

Step 1.
Taking the surface divergence of the boundary conditions we
get by using the differential equation

\displaystyle-\operatorname*{i}k\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}-\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\left(\gamma_{T}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}\right)\overset{\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Nedelec01}{}{}, (2.5.197)]}}}{=}\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}+\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}+\langle\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{n}\rangle=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}+\langle\mathbf{f}-k^{2}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{n}\rangle.

(4.20)

We note that
\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{f}-k^{2}\mathbf{u})=0
so that

\|\langle\mathbf{f}-k^{2}\mathbf{u},\mathbf{n}\rangle\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{f}-k^{2}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}.

(4.21)

Inserting this in (
4.20
) yields

\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C|k|^{-1}\left[\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}+\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\Omega\right)}\right].

(4.22)

It will be convenient to abbreviate

\begin{split}R_{m}:=|k|^{-1}\Bigl[\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+|k|^{-1}\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)}\\
+\left|k\right|^{2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\Omega\right)}+|k|\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\operatorname{curl},\Omega\right)}\Bigr].\end{split}

(4.23)

Step 2.
(
\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
-estimate) With the aid of
Lemma
2.7
(
ii
), we write
\mathbf{u}=\nabla\varphi+\mathbf{z}
with
\varphi\in H^{m+1}(\Omega)
and

\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:Rell})}}{\leq}C|k|^{-1}R_{m},

(4.24)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)}\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:Rell})}}{\leq}CR_{m}.

(4.25)

Step 2a:
We bound

\left\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}\leq C\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{z}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\overset{(\ref{eq:good-sign-101})}{\leq}CR_{m}.

(4.26)

Step 2b:
Applying
\operatorname*{div}_{\Gamma}\Pi_{T}
to the
decomposition of
\mathbf{u}
leads to

\Delta_{\Gamma}\varphi|_{\Gamma}=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}-\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}

(4.27)

with

\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}-\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}\overset{(\ref{difuT_divgT}),(\ref{traceestzt}),(\ref{eq:Rell})}{\leq}CR_{m}.

(4.28)

Together with (
4.27
), we infer

\varphi|_{\Gamma}\in H^{m+3/2}(\Gamma)
. Since
\Gamma
is connected,

\varphi|_{\Gamma}
is unique up to a constant. We select this constant such
that
\varphi|_{\Gamma}
has zero mean. Elliptic regularity implies

\left\|\varphi\right\|_{H^{3/2+m}\left(\Gamma\right)}\leq C\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}-\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{H^{-1/2+m}\left(\Gamma\right)}\overset{(\ref{eq:apriori-with-good-sign-220})}{\leq}CR_{m}.

The function
\varphi
satisfies the following Dirichlet problem:

\Delta\varphi=\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{z}=k^{-2}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{z}\in H^{m}(\Omega),\qquad\varphi|_{\Gamma}\in H^{3/2+m}(\Gamma),

from which we get by elliptic regularity

\left\|\varphi\right\|_{H^{2+m}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|\varphi\|_{H^{3/2+m}(\Gamma)}+\left|k\right|^{-2}\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{z}\right\|_{H^{m}(\Omega)}\right)\leq CR_{m}.

We conclude

\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\leq CR_{m}.

(4.29)

Step 3.
(
\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega)
-estimate) We set
\mathbf{w}:=\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}
. Since

\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
(cf. (
4.29
)) we know that
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)
. As in
Step 2 we write
\mathbf{w}=\nabla\tilde{\varphi}+\mathbf{\tilde{z}}
and
obtain

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\varphi}\|_{H^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\overset{(\ref{eq:uinHm+1})}{\leq}CR_{m},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)}\leq C\left(\|\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{f}-k^{2}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+R_{m}\right).

To estimate the norm of
\tilde{\varphi}
, we employ the boundary condition satisfied by
\mathbf{u}
,
i.e.,

\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}=\mathbf{n}\times\gamma_{T}\nabla\tilde{\varphi}=\mathbf{n}\times\left(\gamma_{T}\mathbf{w}-\gamma_{T}\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\right)=\mathbf{n}\times\left(\mathbf{g}_{T}+\operatorname*{i}k\mathbf{u}_{T}-\gamma_{T}\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\right).

In view of
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)
,
this implies
\tilde{\varphi}|_{\Gamma}\in H^{m+3/2}\left(\Gamma\right)

with

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+\left\|\gamma_{T}\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|R_{m}\right).

The function
\tilde{\varphi}
solves the Dirichlet problem

\Delta\tilde{\varphi}=\operatorname{div}(\mathbf{w}-\mathbf{\tilde{z}})=-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\quad\text{in }\Omega,\qquad\tilde{\varphi}|_{\Gamma}\in H^{m+3/2}\left(\Gamma\right).

(4.30)

Since
\tilde{\varphi}|_{\Gamma}
is determined up to a constant, we may
assume that
\tilde{\varphi}|_{\Gamma}
has vanishing mean. Elliptic
regularity theory for (
4.30
) tells us that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\tilde{\varphi}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left\|\nabla_{\Gamma}\tilde{\varphi}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}^{2}+\left\|\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\right\|_{H^{m}(\Omega)}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|R_{m}\right).

We obtain
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)
with

\displaystyle\left|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{w}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\leq C\left(\left|\nabla\tilde{\varphi}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\left|\nabla\mathbf{\tilde{z}}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|R_{m}\right).

(4.31)

Step 4:
We ascertain the bounds
(
4.18a
),
(
4.18b
) for
m=0
. We have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:item:lemma:apriori-with-good-sign-iia}),
(\ref{eq:H-1div-vs-L2}), (\ref{eq:H-1/2div})}}{\leq}C\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right),

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:H-1/2div})}{\leq}|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\left(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma\right),k}.

This implies for
R_{0}
from (
4.23
)

R_{0}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right)

(4.32)

and in turn from (
4.29
)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\right)\overset{(\ref{eq:uinHm+1}),(\ref{eq:Rell})}{\leq}C|k|^{-1}R_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-3}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right),

which is formula (
4.18a
) for
m=0
. Next,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(|k|^{-1}\|\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+|k|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:uinHm+1curl})}{\leq}C\left(|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}+R_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:R0}),(\ref{eq:H-1div-vs-L2-b})}{\leq}C\left(|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma),k}+\left|k\right|^{-2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}\right),

which is formula (
4.18b
) for
m=0
.

We now assume that the estimates (
4.18a
),
(
4.18b
) holds up to
m
and show that they
hold for
m+1
. Introduce the abbreviations

\displaystyle T_{1}(m)

\displaystyle:=\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k},

\displaystyle T_{2}(m)

\displaystyle:=\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\left|k\right|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma),k}.

It is easy to verify that (using (
4.11
) for the case
m=0
)

T_{1}\left(m\right)\leq CT_{2}\left(m\right)\leq CT_{1}\left(m+1\right).

(4.33)

By the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle|k|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)}\leq C|k|^{m+1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Ind.\ hyp.}}}{{\leq}}C|k|^{m-1}T_{2}(m)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{T1T2est})}}{{\leq}}C|k|^{m-1}T_{1}(m+1).

(4.34)

Hence,

\displaystyle|k|^{-(m+2)}R_{m+1}

\displaystyle=|k|^{-(m+2)}\left(|k|^{-1}\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+|k|^{-2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)}+|k|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{kuT2})}}{{\leq}}C|k|^{-3}\left(\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma),k}+\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+T_{1}(m+1)\right)

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-3}T_{1}(m+1)

(4.35)

and therefore by the induction hypothesis and (
4.29
)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+2}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}+|k|^{-(m+2)}|\mathbf{u}|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{ind.\ hyp., (\ref{eq:uinHm+1})}}{\leq}{C\left(|k|^{-3}T_{1}(m)+|k|^{-(m+2)}R_{m+1}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{estkRm+1})}{\leq}C|k|^{-3}T_{1}(m+1),

(4.36)

which completes the induction step for formula
(
4.18a
).

Again from the definition of
R_{m+1}
, the induction hypothesis, and
(
4.33
), we have

\displaystyle|k|^{-(m+2)}R_{m+1}

\displaystyle\leq C\left[|k|^{-2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+3/2}(\Gamma),k}+|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+|k|^{-3}T_{2}(m)\right]

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-3}T_{2}(m+1).

The combination of this with (
4.36
) and (
4.33
) leads to

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+2}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}\leq C\left(\left|k\right|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+2}(\Omega),k}+|k|^{-(m+2)}|\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\ref{esthHm+2_k}),(\ref{eq:uinHm+1curl})}{\leq}C\left(\left|k\right|^{-2}T_{2}(m+1)+|k|^{-(m+2)}\left(\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+3/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|R_{m+1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left(\left|k\right|^{-2}T_{2}(m+1)+|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+3/2}(\Gamma),k}+\left|k\right|^{-2}T_{2}(m+1)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left|k\right|^{-2}T_{2}(m+1),

which completes the induction argument for
(
4.18b
).

5
Regularity theory for Maxwell’s equations

In this section, we collect regularity assertions for the Maxwell model
problem (
2.43
). In particular, the case of analytic data studied in
Section
5.2
will be a building block for the
regularity by decomposition studied in Section
7
.

5.1
Finite regularity theory

The difference between Maxwell’s equations with the “good” sign and the
time-harmonic Maxwell equations lies in a lower order term. Therefore, higher
regularity statements for the solution of Maxwell’s equations can be inferred
from those for with the “good” sign, i.e., from
Theorem
4.3
. The following result makes this precise.

Lemma 5.1

Let
\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with
simply connected, sufficiently smooth boundary
\Gamma
. Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there is
C>0
(depending only on
m
and
\Omega
) such
that for
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)
,

\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)
the solution
\mathbf{u}
of
(
2.43
) (for
\mathbf{j}:=\mathbf{f}
) satisfies
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)
and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\!\leq C\left[|k|^{-3}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right)+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\!\right],

(5.1)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq\!C\left[\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma),k}\right)+\left|k\right|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\!\right].\!

(5.2)

If assumption (
3.2
) holds, then
\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C|k|^{\theta-1}\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\right)
. In particular,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{-2}\Bigl\{\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+|k|^{\theta+2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

(5.3)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\mbox{}+|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|^{\theta+2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\Bigr\}.

Proof.
The weak solution
\mathbf{u}
of (
2.43
) exists by
Proposition
3.1
and depends continuously on
the data. In particular,
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
. From the
equation
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{f}
, we have
-k^{2}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}=\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}
so that

\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{div},\Omega)
. The function

\mathbf{u}
solves

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,\operatorname*{i}k}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{f}+2k^{2}\mathbf{u},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{g}_{T}.

(5.4)

It is easy to see that Theorem
4.3
is
inductively applicable. We get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}\leq C\left(|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{f}+2k^{2}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\left(|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}\right).

(5.5)

We may successively insert (
5.5
) into itself to
arrive at (
5.1
). The statement
(
5.2
) follows from
(
5.1
) and
Theorem
4.3
and the observation
\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\leq C|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma),k}
.

5.2
Analytic regularity theory

In this section, we consider the Maxwell problem (
2.43
), i.e.,

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{f}\text{ in }\Omega,\qquad\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{g}_{T}\text{ on }\Gamma

(5.6)

with
analytic
data
\mathbf{f}
and
\mathbf{g}_{T}
and analytic
boundary
\Gamma
. We show in Theorem
5.2
that the solution is
analytic, making the dependence on
k
explicit.
In Appendix
A
we generalize the theory in

[
NT20
,
Tom19
]
to the case of inhomogeneous boundary data.
The key idea there is to reformulate the problem
(
5.6
) as an elliptic system and then to apply the
regularity theory for elliptic systems with analytic data to this problem (see

[
CDN10
]
). Here, we summarize the main results.

Problem (
5.6
) can be formulated as an elliptic
system for
\mathbf{U}=\left(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{H}\right)
, where

\mathbf{E}
is the electric and
\mathbf{H}:=-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}
the magnetic field (see Appendix
A
):

\begin{array}[c]{cclc}L\left(\mathbf{U}\right):=&\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}-\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{E}\\
\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{H}-\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{H}\end{array}\right)&=\mathbf{F}+k^{2}\mathbf{U}&\text{in }\Omega,\\
T\left(\mathbf{U}\right):=&\mathbf{H}\times\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{E}_{T}&=-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\mathbf{g}_{T}&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
B\left(\mathbf{U}\right):=&\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{E}\\
\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{H}\\
\gamma_{T}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{H}+\left(\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}\right)_{T}\end{array}\right)&=kG\mathbf{U}+\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}&\text{on }\Gamma\end{array}

(5.7)

for

\mathbf{F}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{f}+\frac{1}{k^{2}}\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}\\
-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right),\text{\quad}G\mathbf{U}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}0\\
0\\
\operatorname*{i}\left(\mathbf{H}_{T}-\gamma_{T}\mathbf{E}\right)\end{array}\right)\text{,\quad}\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\left.\left(\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}\right)\right|_{\Gamma}\\
0\\
-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\gamma_{T}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right).

In Appendix
A
, we show that this system is elliptic in the sense of
[
CDN10
]
. For the
special case
\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}=\mathbf{0}
and
\mathbf{g}_{T}=\mathbf{0}
,
the analytic regularity theory for this problem has been developed in

[
NT20
,
Tom19
]
. The following Theorem
5.2

generalizes their result to the case of inhomogeneous boundary data

\mathbf{g}_{T}
,
\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}
. To describe the analyticity of

\mathbf{g}_{T}
and
\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}
, we assume that these functions are
restrictions of analytic functions
\mathbf{g}^{\ast}
and
\mathbf{G}^{\ast}

on an open neighborhood
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}
of
\Gamma
and satisfy

\mathbf{g}_{T}=\gamma\mathbf{g}^{\ast}
and
\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}=\gamma\mathbf{G}^{\ast}
for the standard trace operator
\gamma
(see (
+1/3
), (
+2/3
)). We write
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{g}},\lambda_{\mathbf{g}},\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\cap\Omega)
if
\mathbf{g}^{\ast}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{g}},\lambda_{\mathbf{g}},\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\cap\Omega)
.

Theorem 5.2

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be a bounded Lipschitz
domain with a simply connected, analytic boundary. Let
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}

be an open neighborhood of
\Gamma
. Let
\mathbf{f}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{f}},\lambda_{\mathbf{f}},\Omega)
and
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{g}},\lambda_{\mathbf{g}},\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\cap\Omega)
. Then there are constants
B
,
C>0
(depending only on
\Omega
,

\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}
, and
\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
,
\lambda_{\mathbf{g}}
)
such that the solution
\mathbf{E}
of (
5.6
)
satisfies

\mathbf{E}\in\mathcal{A}(CC_{\mathbf{E}},B,\Omega),

(5.8)

where
C_{\mathbf{E}}=C_{\mathbf{f}}\left|k\right|^{-2}+C_{{\mathbf{g}}}\left|k\right|^{-1}+\frac{1}{\left|k\right|}\left\|{\mathbf{E}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}
. If
assumption (
3.2
) holds, then

C_{\mathbf{E}}\leq C\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}|k|^{\theta-1}+C_{\mathbf{g}}|k|^{\theta-1/2}\right).

(5.9)

Proof.
The statement of the theorem follows from Corollary
A.2
and more details can be found there.
The existence
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
is
implied by Proposition
3.1
, and finite
regularity assertions for
\mathbf{E}
are provided in
Lemma
5.1
. In particular,
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)
. In turn,
\mathbf{U}=(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{H})\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)
solves the elliptic system (
5.7
).
This makes Theorem
A.1
applicable, which shows the corresponding result
for
\mathbf{U}
by a boot-strapping argument and an explicit tracking of the
wavenumber
k
to arrive at the result of Corollary
A.2
,
which reads

|{\mathbf{E}}|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}\left(\Omega\right)}\leq CC_{\mathbf{E}}B^{p}\max(p,|k|)^{p},\quad\forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}

(5.10)

with
C_{\mathbf{E}}
as given in the statement. A direct calculation shows

\|\mathbf{E}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\mathbf{E}}
and

\|\mathbf{E}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq|k|C_{\mathbf{E}}
so that
(
5.10
) also holds for
p=0
and
p=1
. This shows
(
5.8
).

The estimate (
5.9
) follows from
(
5.3
) of Lemma
5.1
and the
definition of the analyticity classes together with the trace estimates

\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq CC_{\mathbf{g}}|k|
and

\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq CC_{\mathbf{g}}|k|^{1/2}
.

6
Frequency splittings

As in
[
MS10
,
MS11
,
MPS13
,
Mel12
,
EM12
,
MS21a
]
we analyze the regularity of
Maxwell’s equations (
2.43
) via a decomposition of the right-hand side
into high and low frequency parts.

6.1
Frequency splittings in
\Omega
:
H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
,

L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
,
H_{\Omega}
,
L_{\Omega}
,
{H}_{\Omega}^{0}
,

{L}_{\Omega}^{0}

In order to construct the splitting, we start by recalling the definition of
the Fourier transform for sufficiently smooth functions with compact
support

\hat{u}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\mathcal{F}(u)({\boldsymbol{\xi}})=\left(2\pi\right)^{-3/2}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\operatorname{e}^{-\operatorname*{i}\langle{\boldsymbol{\xi}},\mathbf{x}\rangle}u(\mathbf{x})d\mathbf{x}\qquad\forall{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

(6.1)

and the inversion formula

u(\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(u)(\mathbf{x})=\left(2\pi\right)^{-3/2}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}}\operatorname{e}^{\operatorname*{i}\langle\mathbf{x},{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\rangle}\hat{u}({\boldsymbol{\xi}})d{\boldsymbol{\xi}}\qquad\forall\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}.

These formulas extend to tempered distributions and in particular to functions
in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
. Next, we introduce a frequency splitting for
functions in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
that depends on
k
and a parameter
\lambda>1

by using the Fourier transformation. The low and high frequency part is given by

L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\chi_{\lambda\left|k\right|}\mathcal{F}(u)\right)\quad\text{and\quad}H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}u:=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\left(\left(1-\chi_{\lambda\left|k\right|}\right)\mathcal{F}(u)\right),

(6.2)

where
\chi_{\delta}
is the characteristic function of the open ball with
radius
\delta>0
centered at the origin. We note the splitting
H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}+L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}=I
. By using Stein’s extension operator

\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}
,
[
Ste70
, Chap. VI]
this
splitting induces a frequency splitting for functions in Sobolev spaces in

\Omega
via

L_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}:=\left.\left(L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{f}\right)\right|_{\Omega}\quad\text{and\quad}H_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}:=\left.\left(H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{f}\right)\right|_{\Omega},

(6.3)

where, again,
L_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}+H_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}=\mathbf{f}
in

\Omega
.

In general, the condition
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0
neither implies

\operatorname*{div}L_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}=0
nor
\operatorname*{div}H_{\Omega}\mathbf{f}=0
. We therefore introduce another lifting (instead of

\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}
) for functions in Sobolev spaces on

\Omega
that passes on the divergence-free property to the lifting to the full space and
allows for alternative frequency splittings
L_{\Omega}^{0}
,
H_{\Omega}^{0}

at the expense that
L_{\Omega}^{0}+H_{\Omega}^{0}
is not the identity but
the
identity plus a smoothing operator
.
With the operator
\mathbf{R}_{2}
of Lemma
2.6
, which has been
constructed in
[
CM10
]
, we set

H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}:=\operatorname*{curl}H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\quad\text{and\quad}L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}:=\operatorname*{curl}L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}

(6.4)

and define the operator
\mathbf{S}
by

\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f}:\mathbf{=f}-\left.\left(H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}+L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\right)\right|_{\Omega}\mathbf{.}

(6.5)

In view of (
2.27
), we have for
\mathbf{f}
with
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0
that
\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f}=\mathbf{K}_{2}\mathbf{f}|_{\Omega}

so that in particular for all
s
,
s^{\prime}

\|\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Omega)}\leq C_{s,s^{\prime}}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\qquad\forall\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)\colon\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0.

(6.6)

6.2
Frequency splittings on
\Gamma

For the definition of the Hodge decompositions and frequency splittings of
this section, we recall that
\Omega
has a simply connected, analytic boundary.

Remark 6.1

The Laplace-Beltrami operator
\Delta_{\Gamma}
is
self-adjoint with respect to the
L^{2}(\Gamma)
scalar product
\left(\cdot,\cdot\right)_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}
and positive semidefinite. It admits a
countable sequence of eigenfunctions in
L^{2}(\Gamma)
denoted by
Y_{\ell}^{m}
such that

-\Delta_{\Gamma}Y_{\ell}^{m}=\lambda_{\ell}Y_{\ell}^{m}\qquad\text{\ for }\ell=0,1,\ldots\text{and }m\in\iota_{\ell}.

(6.7)

Here,
\iota_{\ell}
is a finite index set whose cardinality equals the
multiplicity of the eigenvalue
\lambda_{\ell}
, and we always assume that the
eigenvalues
\lambda_{\ell}
are distinct and ordered increasingly. We have

\lambda_{0}=0
and for
\ell\geq 1
, they are real and positive and accumulate
at infinity. Since we assumed that
\Gamma
is simply connected we know that

\lambda_{0}=0
is a simple eigenvalue.

According to
[
Néd01
, Sec. 5.4.1]
, any tangential field

\mathbf{h}_{T}\in\mathbf{L}_{T}^{2}(\Gamma)
on the bounded, simply connected
manifold
\Gamma
admits an expansion

\mathbf{h}_{T}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m\in\iota_{\ell}}\alpha_{\ell}^{m}\nabla_{\Gamma}Y_{\ell}^{m}+\beta_{\ell}^{m}\left(\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}Y_{\ell}^{m}\right).

(6.8)

The functions
\left\{\nabla_{\Gamma}Y_{\ell}^{m},\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}Y_{\ell}^{m}:\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},\,m\in\iota_{\ell}\right\}
constitute an orthogonal basis of

\mathbf{L}_{T}^{2}(\Gamma)
and hence the coefficients
\alpha_{\ell}^{m}
,

\beta_{\ell}^{m}
are uniquely determined via (
6.8
). We
set

\begin{array}[c]{ll}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}:={\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{\infty}}{\displaystyle\sum\limits_{m\in\iota_{\ell}}}\alpha_{\ell}^{m}Y_{\ell}^{m},&\qquad\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}:={\displaystyle\sum\limits_{\ell=1}^{\infty}}{\displaystyle\sum\limits_{m\in\iota_{\ell}}}\beta_{\ell}^{m}Y_{\ell}^{m},\\
\Pi_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}:=\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla},&\qquad\Pi_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}:=I-\Pi_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}=\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}},\end{array}

(6.9)

where
I
denotes the identity operator.

Remark 6.2

\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}
are characterized by

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{h}_{T},\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\quad\forall\psi\in C^{\infty}(\Gamma),

(6.10)

\displaystyle\left(\overrightarrow{\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T},\overrightarrow{\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}}\psi\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{h}_{T},\overrightarrow{\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}}\psi\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\quad\forall\psi\in C^{\infty}(\Gamma),

(6.11)

and the conditions
(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T},1)_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}=0
and
(\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T},1)_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}=0
. In strong form, we have in view of
\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}\overrightarrow{\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}}=-\Delta_{\Gamma}
that

\Delta_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}_{T}
and
\Delta_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}=-\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}_{T}
.

In summary, we have introduced a Hodge decomposition:

\mathbf{h}_{T}=\Pi_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}+\Pi_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}=\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi+\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}\psi\quad\text{for }\varphi=\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}\text{ and }\psi=\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}

(6.12)

(for further details see
[
Néd01
, Sec. 5.4.1]
).

Next, we introduce the harmonic extension
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}:H^{1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)\rightarrow H^{1}\left(\Omega\right)
of
Dirichlet boundary data defined by

\Delta\left(\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\varphi\right)=0\quad\text{in
}\Omega\text{, \qquad}\left.\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\varphi\right|_{\Gamma}=\varphi.

(6.13)

(Later, we will use that
\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}
extends to a
continuous operator
H^{s}(\Gamma)\rightarrow H^{1/2+s}(\Omega)
for
s\geq 0
.) This allows us to define boundary frequency filters
L_{\Gamma}
and
H_{\Gamma}

based on this Dirichlet lifting by

L_{\Gamma}\varphi:=\left.\left(L_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\varphi\right)\right|_{\Gamma}\quad\text{and\quad}H_{\Gamma}\varphi:=\left.\left(H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\varphi\right)\right|_{\Gamma}.

(6.14)

The vector-valued versions for tangential fields on the surface are used to define

\begin{array}[c]{ll}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}\left(\mathbf{h}_{T}\right):=\nabla_{\Gamma}\left(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right),&\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}\left(\mathbf{h}_{T}\right):=\nabla_{\Gamma}\left(L_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right),\\
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\left(\mathbf{h}_{T}\right):=\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}\left(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right),&\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\left(\mathbf{h}_{T}\right):=\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}\left(L_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right),\end{array}

(6.15)

and we set

\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}:=\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}+\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\quad\text{and\quad}\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}:=\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}+\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname*{curl}}.

(6.16)

6.3
Estimates for the frequency splittings

Lemma 6.3

Let
\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with simply
connected, analytic boundary. The operators
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}
can
be extended (uniquely) to bounded linear operators
\mathbf{H}_{T}^{s}(\Gamma)\rightarrow H^{s+1}(\Gamma)
for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
and

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}\|_{H^{s+1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}_{T}\|_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma)},

(6.17a)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}\|_{H^{s+1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s}\|\operatorname{curl}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}_{T}\|_{H^{s-1}(\Gamma)}.

(6.17b)

For every
s>-1
, there is
C_{s}>0
independent of
\lambda>1
such that for
any
\mathbf{h}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{s}(\Gamma)
there holds

\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}_{T}=\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}+\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}
together with

\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Gamma)}+\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{h}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}\left(\Gamma\right)}\leq C_{s}\left\|\mathbf{h}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}\left(\Gamma\right)}.

Proof.
The mapping properties for
\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\operatorname{curl}}
, follow directly from
elliptic regularity theory on smooth manifolds in view of
Remark
6.2
. For the stability of the operators

\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\nabla}
,
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname{curl}}
we
use the stability of the operator
H_{\Omega}:H^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)\rightarrow H^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)
for
s^{\prime}\geq 0
and the stability
of the trace operator
\gamma:H^{1/2+s^{\prime}}(\Omega)\rightarrow H^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)
for
s^{\prime}>0
as in
[
MS11
, Lem. 4.2]
to get that
\mathbf{h}_{T}\mapsto\gamma H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{h}_{T}
maps continuously
H^{-1+\varepsilon}(\Gamma)\rightarrow H^{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)
for any
\varepsilon>0
with continuity constant
independent of
\lambda>1
. Since
\nabla_{\Gamma}:H^{\varepsilon}(\Gamma)\rightarrow\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1+\varepsilon}(\Gamma)
, the result
follows. The case of
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}^{\operatorname{curl}}
is handled analogously.

We recall some properties of the high frequency splittings that are proved in

[
MS11
, Lem. 4.2]
.

Proposition 6.4

Let
\lambda>1
be the parameter
appearing in the definition of
H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
in (
6.2
).
Let
H_{\Omega}
,
H_{\Omega}^{0}
, and
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}
be the operators
of (
6.3
), (
6.4
), and (
6.16
).
There are constants
C_{s^{\prime},s}
independent of
\lambda>1
such that
the following holds.

(i)

The frequency splitting
(
6.2
) satisfies for all
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
the
estimates

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}f\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\qquad\forall f\in H^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3}),

(6.18)

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\Omega}f\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|f\right\|_{H^{s}(\Omega)}\qquad\forall f\in H^{s}(\Omega),

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Omega)}\qquad\forall\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Omega).

(6.20)

These estimates hold also for Lipschitz domains.

(ii)

Let
0\leq s^{\prime}<s
or
0<s^{\prime}\leq s
. Then the operator
H_{\Gamma}
satisfies

\left\|H_{\Gamma}g\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\|g\|_{H^{s}(\Gamma)}.

(6.21)

(iii)

Let
-1\leq s^{\prime}<s
or
-1<s^{\prime}\leq s
. Then the operator
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}

satisfies for
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{s}(\Gamma)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Gamma)},

(6.22a)

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s-1}(\Gamma)},

(6.22b)

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-1}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\|\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s-1}(\Gamma)}.

(6.22c)

Proof.

Proof of (
i
):
Estimates
(
6.18
) and (
6.19
) are shown in
[
MS11
, Lem. 4.2]
. To see (
6.20
), we bound
H_{\Omega}^{0}
as
follows

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\operatorname*{curl}H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\Omega)}\leq\left\|H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\operatorname*{curl}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{hfsplitdomain})}}{\leq}C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s+1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s+1}(\Omega)}\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}(\Omega)}.

Proof of (
ii
):
For

s^{\prime}>0
the definition of
H_{\Gamma}
in (
6.14
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|H_{\Gamma}g\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle=\left\|H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}g\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}(\Gamma)}\leq C\left\|H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}g\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}+1/2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{4.3})}}{\leq}\tilde{C}_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}g\right\|_{H^{s+1/2}(\Omega)}.

The regularity theory for the Laplace problem (
6.13
) leads
to (
6.21
). For the case
s^{\prime}=0
, we have
s>0
, and the multiplicative trace inequality

\|H_{\Gamma}g\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}g\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{1-1/(2s+1)}\|H_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}g\|_{H^{s+1/2}(\Omega)}^{1/(2s+1)},

together with the properties of
H_{\Omega}
lead to the result.

Proof of (
iii
):
We have

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}=\nabla_{\Gamma}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T})+\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{g}_{T}).

A triangle inequality leads to

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s^{\prime}+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:lemma:properties-of-H-via-lifting-1000})}}{\leq}C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left(\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{eq:LemScLift})}}{\leq}C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{s}\left(\Gamma\right)},

which shows (
6.22a
). For (
6.22b
) we start from

\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}=\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\left(\nabla_{\Gamma}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T})+\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{g}_{T})\right)=\Delta_{\Gamma}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}).

We apply the previous estimate
(
6.21
) to get

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-1}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\Delta_{\Gamma}(H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T})\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-1}\left(\Gamma\right)}\leq C\left\|H_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq C_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s+1}\left(\Gamma\right)}.

From (
6.10
), we obtain
\Delta_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}=\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}
so that

\displaystyle\left\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s^{\prime}-1}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\Delta_{\Gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s-1}\left(\Gamma\right)}

\displaystyle=\tilde{C}_{s^{\prime},s}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{s^{\prime}-s}\left\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{s-1}\left(\Gamma\right)}.

This shows (
6.22b
). The proof of (
6.22c
) follows
along the same lines by using
\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\overrightarrow{\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}}=-\Delta_{\Gamma}
and

\operatorname*{curl}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}=0
.

The following lemma concerns the parameter-explicit bounds for the low
frequency operators.

Lemma 6.5

Let
\lambda>1
be fixed in the definition
(
6.2
) of
L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}
. There exists a constant
C>0

independent of
\lambda
such that for all
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
,
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
, there holds

\displaystyle\bigl|L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{v}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lambda|k|\right)^{p}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})},

(6.23a)

\displaystyle\bigl|L_{\Omega}\mathbf{w}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\Omega)}+\bigl|L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{w}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lambda|k|\right)^{p}\left\|\mathbf{w}\right\|.

(6.23b)

For the boundary frequency filter we have, due to the analyticity of
\Gamma
,
the existence of
C>0
and a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
of
\Gamma
(depending only on
\Omega
) and some
\gamma>0

(depending additionally on
\lambda
) such that for each
\mathbf{z}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)
there exists a function

\mathbf{Z}\in\mathcal{A}\bigl(C\left\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)},\gamma,\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\bigr)

such that
\mathbf{Z}|_{\Gamma}=L_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}
.

Proof.
From
[
MS10
, (3.32b)]
for the full space and from

[
MS11
, Lem. 4.3]
for bounded domains the estimates
(
6.23a
) and the first one in (
6.23b
) follow. For the
operator
L_{\Omega}^{0}
, recall the lifting operator
\mathbf{R}_{2}
of
(
2.6
). Then, for any
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)
and
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the second estimate in
(
6.23b
) follows from

\displaystyle\bigl|L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{w}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl|L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{w}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\left|\operatorname*{curl}L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{w}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}=\left|L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\operatorname*{curl}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{w}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{freqfiltesta})}{\leq}C(\lambda|k|)^{p}\left\|\operatorname{curl}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{w}\right\|\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{p}\left\|\mathbf{w}\right\|.

Finally, we consider the boundary low frequency operator. For
\mathbf{z}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)
, we define functions in the volume
\Omega

via

\Phi:=L_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T},\qquad\Psi:=L_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{z}_{T}

so that,
for
{\boldsymbol{\phi}}:=\nabla\Phi
and
{\boldsymbol{\psi}}:=\nabla\Psi
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{T}\nabla\Phi+\Pi_{T}\nabla\Psi\times\mathbf{n}=\mathbf{n}\times\left(\left.{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\right|_{\Gamma}\times\mathbf{n}\right)+\left.{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\right|_{\Gamma}\times\mathbf{n}.

Let
\mathbf{n}^{\ast}
denote an analytic extension of the
normal vector field into the domain
\Omega
; due to the analyticity of the
domain we may assume that there are constants
C_{\mathbf{n}}
,
\gamma_{\mathbf{n}}>0
and a tubular neighborhood
\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\subset\Omega
with
\Gamma\subset\overline{\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}}

such that
\mathbf{n}^{\ast}\in\mathcal{A}^{\infty}\left(C_{\mathbf{n}},\gamma_{\mathbf{n}},\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\right)
. Let

\mathbf{N}_{\ast}:=\left[\begin{array}[c]{ccc}0&n_{3}^{\ast}&-n_{2}^{\ast}\\
-n_{3}^{\ast}&0&n_{1}^{\ast}\\
n_{2}^{\ast}&-n_{1}^{\ast}&0\end{array}\right].

Then,

\left.\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}\right|_{\Gamma}\times\mathbf{n=}\left(\mathbf{N}_{\ast}\mbox{\boldmath$\psi$}\right)_{\Gamma}\quad\text{and\quad}\mathbf{n}\times\left(\left.\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}\right|_{\Gamma}\times\mathbf{n}\right)=-\left(\mathbf{N}_{\ast}^{2}\mbox{\boldmath$\phi$}\right)_{\Gamma},

i.e.,

\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{z}_{T}=\left.\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{z}}\right|_{\Gamma}\quad\text{for }\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{z}}:=\mathbf{N}_{\ast}\left({\boldsymbol{\psi}}-\mathbf{N}_{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\right).

(6.24)

We further have for
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle\left|{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma})}

\displaystyle=\bigl|\nabla L_{\Omega}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma})}\leq\bigl|L_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nabla\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{p}\bigl\|\nabla\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname*{Stein}}\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{p}\bigl\|\mathcal{E}_{\Omega}^{\Delta}\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\lambda|k|\right)^{p}\bigl\|\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\nabla}\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C_{1}\left(\lambda|k|\right)^{p}\bigl\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\bigr\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}.

The proof of the estimate

\left|{\boldsymbol{\psi}}\right|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}\left(\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\right)}\leq C_{2}\left(\lambda\left|k\right|\right)^{p}\left\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}

follows along the same lines. Next we use
[
Mel02
, Lem. 4.3.1]
(an
inspection of the proof shows that this lemma also holds for
d=3
) to deduce
that
\mathbf{N}_{\ast}{\boldsymbol{\psi}}
,
-\mathbf{N}_{\ast}^{2}{\boldsymbol{\phi}}\in\mathcal{A}\bigl(C\left\|\mathbf{z}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)},\gamma,\mathcal{U}_{\Gamma}\bigr)
,
where
C
depends only on
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
,
C_{\mathbf{n}}
,

\gamma_{\mathbf{n}}
, while
\gamma
depends additionally on
\lambda
.

7
k
-Explicit regularity by decomposition

In this section, we always assume that the bounded Lipschitz domain

\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
has a simply connected, analytic boundary

\Gamma=\partial\Omega
. We consider the Maxwell problem (
2.43
) with
data
\mathbf{f}
,
\mathbf{g}_{T}
with finite regularity.

For the regularity analysis of the operator
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname*{MW}}
it is key to understand that the solutions for high
frequency right-hand sides have low order regularity but well-behaved
stability constant (with respect to the wavenumber) while solutions
corresponding to low-frequency right-hand sides are analytic but with possibly
growing stability constant. This different behavior is reflected in the
regularity theory, which decomposes the solution
\mathbf{z}=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})
into a part with
finite regularity that can be controlled uniformly in
k
and an analytic part
that can be controlled explicitly in
k
. This is achieved in
Theorem
7.3
. The main idea of the proof is to exploit that the
operators
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}
and
\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,\operatorname{i}k}
have the same leading order differential operator. With the filter
operators of the preceding Section
6
and recalling

I=H_{\Omega}^{0}+L_{\Omega}^{0}+\mathbf{S}=H_{\Omega}^{0}+L_{\Omega}^{0}+H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}+L_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}
as well as
I=\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}+\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}
one can write

\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}+H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})+\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}+L_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})+\mathbf{z}^{\prime}

for a remainder
\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
. One then makes the following observations:

1.

If
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0
(which may be achieved by
subtracting a suitable gradient field), then the operator
\mathbf{S}
is
smoothing by (
6.6
).

2.

The term
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}+H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})
has finite
regularity properties given by Theorem
4.3
. The
effect of the high frequency filters
H_{\Omega}^{0}
and
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}
is that they improve the
k
-dependence of lower-order terms in the indexed
norms such as
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}
(see
Lemma
7.1
below).

3.

\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(L_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}+L_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})
is an
analytic function and can be estimated with the aid of
Theorem
5.2
.

4.

The function
\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}\mathbf{z}^{\prime}=\mathbf{r},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}=0,

where, by suitably choosing the cut-off parameters
\lambda
in the frequency
operators, the residual
\mathbf{r}
satisfies
\|\mathbf{r}\|_{\ast}\leq q\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\ast}
for some
q\in(0,1)
and a suitable norm

\|\cdot\|_{\ast}
. Hence, the arguments can be repeated for

\mathbf{z}^{\prime}
and the decomposition can be obtained by a geometric
series argument.

7.1
The concatenation of
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}
with high
frequency filters

The following lemma analyzes the mapping properties of the concatenation of
the solution operator
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}
with the high frequency
filter operators
H_{\Omega}^{0}
and
\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}
.

Lemma 7.1

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
Provided the right-hand sides are finite, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle|k|^{m}\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)},

(7.1)

\displaystyle|k|^{m-1}\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)},\qquad m\geq 1,

(7.2)

\displaystyle{|k|}\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(7.3)

\displaystyle|k|^{m+2}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}({H}_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f},0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)},

(7.4)

as well as

\displaystyle|k|^{m-1}\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}\leq

(7.5)

\displaystyle\quad C(\lambda|k|)^{-\ell}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}\right),

\displaystyle|k|^{m+2}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}\leq

(7.6)

\displaystyle\quad C(\lambda|k|)^{-\ell}\begin{cases}\lambda^{-1}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}\right),&m\geq 1,\\
\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}\right),&m=0.\end{cases}

For
\mathbf{f}\in\mathbf{L^{2}}(\Omega)
with
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0
and the operator
\mathbf{S}
of (
6.5
) we have for any

n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle|k|^{n}\|H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}\lambda^{-n}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},

(7.7)

\displaystyle|k|^{n}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f},0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}\lambda^{-n}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(7.8)

Proof.

Proof of (
7.1
):

(
7.1
)
follows from the fact that
\operatorname{div}H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}=0

and Proposition
6.4
.

Proof of (
7.2
):

For
m\geq 1
, we estimate

\displaystyle\|{H}_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1}(\Omega),k}\leq C\left(\|{H}_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+|k|^{-(m-1)}|{H}_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\text{Prop.~\ref{lemma:properties-of-H-via-lifting}}}{\leq}C\left((\lambda|k|)^{-m}+|k|^{-(m-1)}(\lambda|k|)^{-1}\right)\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)}\leq C\lambda^{-1}|k|^{-m}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}.

Noting that
\operatorname{div}H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}=0
, the estimate
(
7.2
) follows.

Proof of (
7.3
):

Recall
the definition of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}
in (
4.3
) and observe with the multiplicative trace
inequality

\|\gamma_{T}H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{-1/2}\|\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{3})}\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

(7.9)

Since
H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}=\operatorname{curl}H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{Stein}}\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{f}
, we get with an
integration by parts (
[
Mon03
, Thm. 3.29]
) for
{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{X}}_{\operatorname{imp}}

\displaystyle\left|(H_{\Omega}^{0}{\mathbf{f}},{\mathbf{v}})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|(\gamma_{T}H_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{Stein}}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}{\mathbf{f}},{\mathbf{v}}_{T})_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+(H_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}{\mathcal{E}}_{\operatorname{Stein}}{\mathbf{R}}_{2}{\mathbf{f}},\operatorname{curl}{\mathbf{v}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C(\lambda|k|)^{-1/2}\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|{\mathbf{v}}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}+(\lambda|k|)^{-1}\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\|\operatorname{curl}{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}|k|^{-1}\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{{\mathbf{L}}^{2}(\Omega)}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}.

We conclude

\displaystyle\left|k\right|^{1}\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
by the definition (
4.3
)
of
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}
,
and the statement (
7.3
) is shown.

Proof of (
7.4
):
For
m\geq 1
, we obtain
(
7.4
) from (
7.2
) and
Theorem
4.3
,
(
4.18a
). For
m=0
,
we observe that
Theorem
4.3
,
(
4.17
) and
(
7.3
) imply
\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f},0)\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq|k|^{-1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f},0)\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}\leq C{|k|^{-1}}\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}(\Omega),k}\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}
.

Proof of (
7.5
):
We distinguish the cases

m=0
and
m\geq 1
. For
m\geq 1
, the statement follows from the
estimates
of
{\mathbf{H}}_{\Gamma}
given in
Proposition
6.4
. For
m=0
, in addition to
Proposition
6.4
one invokes
Lemma
4.2
.

Proof of (
7.6
):
For
m\geq 1
, the estimate
(
7.6
) follows from combining
Proposition
6.4
with
(
4.18a
)
and (
7.5
) (taking
\ell+1
for
\ell
there) to get
