\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega),k}\leq C|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{(\ref{eq:lemma:estimateS+H-15})}{\leq}C|k|^{-3-m+2}(\lambda|k|)^{-1-\ell}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{m-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}\right).

For
m=0
, we use Theorem
4.3
,
(
4.17
) to get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq C|k|^{-1}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}\leq C|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{\prime}\left(\Gamma\right),k}

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\text{(\ref{estgTprime})}}{\leq}C\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\overset{\text{Prop.~\ref{lemma:properties-of-H-via-lifting}}}{\leq}C\left|k\right|^{-2}(\lambda|k|)^{-\ell}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}+\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{{H}^{-1/2+\ell}(\Gamma)}\right).

This completes the proof of (
7.6
).

Proof of (
7.7
), (
7.8
):
For
\mathbf{f}
with
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}=0
we have

\mathbf{S}\mathbf{f}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega)
by (
6.6
). Hence, (
7.7
), (
7.8
)
follow from Proposition
6.4
and
(
6.6
) and again
Theorem
4.3
.

Lemma 7.2

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\in H^{m-1}(\Omega)
,
\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\in H^{m-3/2}(\Gamma)
. Then there exist
\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\in H^{m+1}(\Omega)\cap H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
and
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\in H^{m+1}(\Omega)

such that for
\ell=0,\ldots,m

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\|_{H^{\ell+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell-1}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle-\operatorname{div}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\|_{H^{\ell+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell-3/2}(\Gamma)},

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}.

Proof.
Define
\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\in H_{0}^{1}(\Omega)
as the weak solution of

-\Delta\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}=\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}.

By elliptic regularity, we have
\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\in H^{m+1}(\Omega)

and the stated bounds. The function
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
is defined in two
steps: First, let
\varphi\in H^{1}(\Gamma)
with
\int_{\Gamma}\varphi=0

denote the weak solution of

\Delta_{\Gamma}\varphi=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T},

which satisfies
\|\varphi\|_{H^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{H^{m-3/2}(\Gamma)}
. Then,

\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
is defined on
\Omega
as the harmonic extension from

\Gamma
, i.e.,
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\in H^{m+1}(\Omega)
solves

\Delta\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}=0,\qquad\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}|_{\Gamma}=\varphi.

Again, the bounds follow from elliptic regularity theory.

7.2
Regularity by decomposition: the main result

Theorem 7.3

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded Lipschitz
domain with a simply connected, analytic boundary
\Gamma=\partial\Omega
. Let
the stability Assumption
3.2
be satisfied. Then there is
a linear mapping
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{H}_{T}^{-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma)\ni(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})\mapsto(\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}},\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}},\varphi_{\mathbf{f}},\varphi_{\mathbf{g}})
such that the solution
\mathbf{z}:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname{imp}}
of (
2.43
) can be written as
\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}+k^{-2}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}+\operatorname{i}k^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
.

The linear mapping has the following properties: For any
m
,
m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there are constants
C
,
B>0
(depending only on
\Omega

and
m
,
m^{\prime}
) such that for
(\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}_{T})\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)
with

(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f},\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\in H^{m^{\prime}-1}(\Omega)\times H^{m^{\prime}-3/2}(\Gamma)
the following holds:

(i)

The function
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}
satisfies

\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}\leq C|k|^{-m-2}\left(|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}+\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\right).

(7.10)

If
\mathbf{g}_{T}\in\mathbf{H}_{T}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)
, then

\left\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-m-1}\left(\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\Omega\right)}\right)

(7.11)

and in (
7.10
) the term
|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}
can be replaced with
\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}
.

(ii)

The gradient fields
\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}
and
\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
are given by
Lemma
7.2
and satisfy, for
\ell=0,\ldots,m^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{H^{\ell-1}\left(\Omega\right)},

(7.12)

\displaystyle\left\|\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{H^{\ell-3/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}.

(7.13)

(iii)

The analytic part
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}

satisfies

\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}\in\mathcal{A}(C|k|^{\theta-1}\{\|\mathbf{f}\|+|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\},B,\Omega).

(7.14)

Proof.
By linearity of the solution operator
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}
, we consider the cases
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f},0)
and
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(0,\mathbf{g}_{T})
separately. The fact that the
right-hand sides in (
7.10
), (
7.11
),
(
7.14
) do not contain the divergence of
\mathbf{f}
or

\mathbf{g}_{T}
is due to the fact that we suitably choose the functions

\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}
,
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
in the course of the proof.

Step 1:
(reduction to divergence-free data) Let the functions

\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}
,
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
be given by
Lemma
7.2
. These functions have the regularity
properties given in (
ii
). The function
\mathbf{z}^{\prime}:=\mathbf{z}-k^{-2}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}-\operatorname{i}k^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}\mathbf{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{f}+\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}+\operatorname{i}k\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}=:\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\quad\mbox{in $\Omega$},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\mathbf{z}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{g}_{T}-\nabla_{\Gamma}\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}=:\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\quad\mbox{on $\Gamma$}.

By construction,
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}^{\prime}=0
and

\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}=0
. Furthermore, using

\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}
and
\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-3/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}
we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+C|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}\right),

(7.15)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}.

(7.16)

Step 2:
(Analysis of
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f}^{\prime},0)
with
\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}^{\prime}=0
) We
claim that

\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f}^{\prime},0)={\mathbf{z}}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}}}+{\mathbf{z}}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathbf{f}}}

(7.17)

for some functions
{\mathbf{z}}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}}}
and
{\mathbf{z}}_{{\mathcal{A}},{\mathbf{f}}}
satisfying the estimates (
7.11
)
(and therefore also (
7.10
) since we focus on the case
{\mathbf{g}}_{T}=0
and (
7.14
). We have

\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}^{\prime}=0
and assume
\mathbf{g}_{T}=0
, which
implies
\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}=0
. Set
\mathbf{f}_{0}^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{0}:=\mathbf{f}^{\prime}
and define, with the
mapping
\mathbf{f}\mapsto\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}
of
Lemma
7.2
, recursively for
n=0,1,\ldots,

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n},0)+\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n},0)

(7.18)

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},n}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(L_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n},0)+\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(L_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n},0)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=2k^{2}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n+1}

\displaystyle:=\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}+\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}}.

We note that
\operatorname{div}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}=0
for all
n
.
From Lemma
7.1
, we get:
if
\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\in\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)
, then

|k|^{\ell+2}\|\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}({H}_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n},0)\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega),k}\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)}.

(7.19)

Next, we obtain from Lemma
7.2
and the above defined recurrence relation

\displaystyle\|\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell-1}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)},\qquad\ell=0,\ldots,m,

(7.20)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{Splusauxest})}}{\leq}C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)}\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\ell}(\Omega)},\ \ell=0,\ldots,m,

(7.21)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n+1}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\leq C\lambda^{-1/2}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}.

(7.22)

From the equation that defines
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n}
and since

\operatorname*{div}H_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}=0
we get

(2k^{2})^{-1}\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}=\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n}=k^{-2}\operatorname*{div}H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}.

(7.23)

Since
\mathbf{S}
is a smoothing operator, this implies that

\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}
is smooth and
the first estimate in
(
7.20
) actually holds for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.
The bounds (
7.21
), (
7.22
) show that the
functions
\mathbf{f}_{n}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}
decay in geometric
progression as
n
increases if
\lambda>1
is chosen such that
C\lambda^{-1/2}=:q<1
. Fixing such a
\lambda>1
, a geometric series argument implies
for any
\mu\in\left\{0,1,\ldots,m\right\}

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\mu}(\Omega)}\leq C\|\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{\mu}(\Omega)}.

(7.24)

We also get from Theorem
4.3
and
Lemma
7.1
and the smoothing property of
{\mathbf{S}}

(recall that
\operatorname*{div}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}=0
)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\!\!\overset{\text{Thm.~\ref{lemma:apriori-with-good-sign}}}{\leq}\!\!C\left|k\right|^{-2}\left(\|H_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}+\|H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\operatorname{div},\Omega),k}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:lemma:estimateS+H-3})}{\leq}C|k|^{-(m+1)}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}.

(7.25)

Lemma
6.5
shows that
L_{\Omega}^{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}
,
L_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},C_{2}\lambda|k|,\Omega)
for some
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
depending only on
\Omega
. From Theorem
5.2
, we infer

|\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},n}|_{\mathbf{H}^{p}(\Omega)}\leq C_{\mathbf{z}}|k|^{\theta-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\gamma^{p}\max\,(p,|k|)^{p}\qquad\forall p\in\mathbb{N}_{p\geq 2}

(7.26)

for some
C_{\mathbf{z}}
,
\gamma
independent of
k
and
n
;
\gamma

depends on
\lambda
, which has been fixed above. Upon setting

\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}^{\prime}}}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},n},\qquad\nabla\varphi:=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}},\qquad\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},n},

we have by (
7.25
) and (
7.24
)

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}^{\prime}}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-(m+1)}\|\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}\leq C|k|^{-(m+1)}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)},

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{A}(C|k|^{\theta-1}\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},\gamma,\Omega).

For the term
\nabla\varphi
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|k^{-2}\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle=\left|k\right|^{-1}\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left\|\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\Omega\right),k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{rep_divfnprime})}}{=}C\left|k\right|^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\|2\operatorname*{div}H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}\left(\Omega\right),k}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\|H_{\Omega}\mathbf{S}\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\Omega\right),k}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-\left(m+1\right)}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{f}}_{n}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Omega\right)},

where we used (
7.7
) with
n\leftarrow m+1
for the
last estimate. The combination with (
7.24
) shows

\|k^{-2}\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}\leq C\left|k\right|^{-\left(m+1\right)}\|\mathbf{f}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}.

We set

{\mathbf{z}}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}}}:={\mathbf{z}}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}}^{\prime}}+k^{-2}\nabla\varphi.

That is, the terms
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{f}}
and
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}}
satisfy the estimates
(
7.10
), (
7.11
) given in the statement of the theorem
for the present case
{\mathbf{g}}_{T}=0
.
We compute

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}(\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{f}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}})=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathbf{f}_{n}^{\prime}-\mathbf{f}_{n+1}^{\prime}=\mathbf{f}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{f}^{\prime},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}(\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},{\mathbf{f}}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}})=0.

By the uniqueness assertion of Proposition
3.1
, we have identified
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{f}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}}=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(\mathbf{f}^{\prime},0)
.

Step 3:
(Analysis of
\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(0,\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime})
with
\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}=0
) We define

\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}),\qquad\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{g}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(0,\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}).

From Theorem
4.3
and the properties of

\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}
given in
Proposition
6.4
we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-3}\|\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma},\Gamma),k}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:lemma:estimateS+H-15}),\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}=0}{\leq}C|k|^{-1-m}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)},

(7.27)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Prop.~\ref{lemma:properties-of-H-via-lifting}}}{\leq}C|k|^{-(m+1)}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}.

(7.28)

That is,
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}
satisfies the estimates
(
7.10
), (
7.11
) given in the
statement of the theorem for the present case
{\mathbf{f}}=0
.
For
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{g}}
we observe
that Lemma
6.5
ensures
7
7

7

We write
\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}
instead of introducing a new symbol
\mathbf{Z}

with
\mathbf{Z}|_{\Gamma}=\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}

\mathbf{L}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}(C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)},\gamma,\Omega)
for some
C

depending only on
\Omega
and
\gamma>0
depending on
\Omega
and
\lambda
.
We note
\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}
by
(
7.16
). From Theorem
5.2
we obtain

\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{g}}\in\mathcal{A}(C|k|^{\theta-1/2}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)},\gamma,\Omega).

Since
\theta-1/2\leq\theta
, the function
\mathbf{z}_{\mathbf{A},\mathbf{g}}
satisfies the estimate
stated in the theorem. Finally, we observe that
\widehat{\mathbf{z}}:=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{\operatorname{MW}}(0,\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime})-(\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{g}})

satisfies

\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,k}\widehat{\mathbf{z}}=2k^{2}\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,k}^{+}(0,\mathbf{H}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime})=:\widehat{\mathbf{f}},\qquad\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,k}\widehat{\mathbf{z}}=0.

From Lemma
7.1
we get using
\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}=0

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)},

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)},

(7.29a)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}.

(7.29b)

We note that
\operatorname{div}\widehat{\mathbf{f}}=0
and that Step 2
provides a decomposition of
\widehat{\mathbf{z}}
in the form
\widehat{\mathbf{z}}=\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}
. By Step 2, the term
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}
can be controlled in terms of
\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}
in thus in the required form. For

\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}
, we note that Step 2 yields

\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}}\in\mathcal{A}(C|k|^{\theta-1}\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)},\gamma,\Omega)\subset\mathcal{A}(C|k|^{\theta}\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)},\gamma,\Omega),

which is an analytic function with the desired estimate. We summarize that

\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}
,
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}
in the statement of the
theorem are given by

\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}=\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{f}}+\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}+\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}\quad\text{and\quad}\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{f}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{g}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A},\widehat{\mathbf{f}}}

and the summands have been estimated in Step 1-3.

Step 4:
The proof is now complete with the exception of the statement
in (
i
) that
|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)}
can be replaced with
\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}
. However, this follows directly from
(
7.11
) via

\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\Omega\right),k}\leq\left\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2},\mathbf{g}}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}\overset{\text{(\ref{ZH2gsecond})}}{\leq}C\left|k\right|^{-m-1}\left\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}

and for the control of
\widehat{\mathbf{z}}
in Step 3 via the bound

\|\widehat{\mathbf{f}}\|_{{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{g}}_{T}^{\prime}\|_{{\mathbf{H}^{m+1/2}(\Gamma)}}
in
(
7.29
).

8
Discretization

In this section, we describe the
hp
-FEM based on Nédélec elements
and discuss the approximation properties of various
hp
-approximation operators.
These operators made their appearance already in
[
MS21a
]
. Here,
we strengthen the results of
[
MS21a
, Sec. 8]
in that we additionally
control the error on the boundary of the elements, which is required due to the
impedance boundary conditions considered here.

8.1
Meshes and Nédélec elements

The classical example of curl-conforming FE spaces are the Nédélec
elements,
[
Néd80
]
. We restrict our attention here to so-called
“type I” elements (sometimes also referred
to as the Nédélec-Raviart-Thomas element) on tetrahedra. These spaces
are based on a conforming (no hanging nodes), shape-regular triangulation
{\mathcal{T}}_{h}
of
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
. That is,
{\mathcal{T}}_{h}
satisfies:

(i)

The (open) elements
K\in{\mathcal{T}}_{h}
cover
\Omega
, i.e.,

\overline{\Omega}=\cup_{K\in{\mathcal{T}}_{h}}\overline{K}
.

(ii)

Associated with each element
K
is the
element map
, a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism
F_{K}:\overline{\widehat{K}}\rightarrow\overline{K}
. The
set
\widehat{K}
is the
reference tetrahedron
.

(iii)

Denoting
h_{K}=\operatorname*{diam}K
, there holds, with some

shape-regularity constant
\gamma_{\mathcal{T}}
,

h_{K}^{-1}\|F_{K}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}+h_{K}\|(F_{K}^{\prime})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}\leq\gamma_{\mathcal{T}}.

(8.1)

(iv)

The intersection of two elements is only empty, a vertex, an edge, a
face, or they coincide (here, vertices, edges, and faces are the images of the
corresponding entities on the reference tetrahedron
\widehat{K}
). The
parametrization of common edges or faces are compatible. That is, if two
elements
K
,
K^{\prime}
share an edge (i.e.,
F_{K}(e)=F_{K^{\prime}}(e^{\prime})
for edges
e
,
e^{\prime}
of
\widehat{K}
) or a face (i.e.,

F_{K}(f)=F_{K^{\prime}}(f^{\prime})
for faces
f
,
f^{\prime}
of

\widehat{K}
), then
F_{K}^{-1}\circ F_{K^{\prime}}:f^{\prime}\rightarrow f

is an affine isomorphism.

The maximal mesh width is denoted by

h:=\max\left\{h_{K}:K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}\right\}.

(8.2)

The following assumption
requires
that the element map
F_{K}
can be decomposed as a composition of an affine
scaling with an
h
-independent mapping. We adopt the setting of

[
MS10
, Sec. 5]
and assume that the element maps
F_{K}

of the conforming,
\gamma
-shape regular triangulation
{\mathcal{T}}_{h}
satisfy the
following additional requirements:

Assumption 8.1
(normalizable regular triangulation)

Each element map

F_{K}
can be written as
F_{K}=R_{K}\circ A_{K}
, where
A_{K}
is an

affine
map and the maps
R_{K}
and
A_{K}
satisfy for constants

C_{\operatorname*{affine}}
,
C_{\operatorname{metric}}
,
\gamma>0

independent of
K
:

\displaystyle\|A_{K}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}\leq C_{\operatorname*{affine}}h_{K},\qquad\|(A_{K}^{\prime})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}\leq C_{\operatorname*{affine}}h_{K}^{-1},

\displaystyle\|(R_{K}^{\prime})^{-1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{K})}\leq C_{\operatorname{metric}},\qquad\|\nabla^{n}R_{K}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widetilde{K})}\leq C_{\operatorname{metric}}\gamma^{n}n!\qquad\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

Here,
\widetilde{K}=A_{K}(\widehat{K})
and
h_{K}>0
is the element diameter.

Remark 8.2

A prime example of meshes that satisfy Assumption
8.1
are
those patchwise structured meshes as described, for example, in

[
MS10
, Ex. 5.1]
or
[
Mel02
, Sec. 3.3.2]
.
These meshes are obtained by first fixing a macro triangulation of
\Omega
;
the actual triangulation is then obtained as images of affine triangulations
of the reference element.

On the reference tetrahedron
\widehat{K}
we introduce the classical
Nédélec type I and Raviart-Thomas elements of degree
p\geq 0
(see,
e.g.,
[
Mon03
]
):

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\widehat{K})

\displaystyle:=\operatorname*{span}\{\mathbf{x}^{\alpha}\,|\,|\alpha|\leq p\},

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{RT}_{p}(\widehat{K})

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{p}(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{x}{q}(\mathbf{x})\,|\,\mathbf{p}\in({\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\widehat{K}))^{3},{q}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\widehat{K})\},

(8.4)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\widehat{K})

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{p}(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{x}\times\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{x})\,|\,\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q}\in({\mathcal{P}}_{p}(\widehat{K}))^{3}\}.

(8.5)

The spaces
S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})
,
\mathbf{RT}_{p}\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}\right)
,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})
are then defined as in
[
Mon03
, (3.76)]
by
transforming covariantly
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\widehat{K})
and contravariantly
\mathbf{RT}_{p}({\widehat{K}})
:

\displaystyle S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})

\displaystyle:=\{u\in H^{1}(\Omega)\,|\,u|_{K}\circ F_{K}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p+1}(\widehat{K})\},

(8.6a)

\displaystyle\mathbf{RT}_{p}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{div},\Omega)\,|\,({\operatorname*{det}F_{K}^{\prime}})(F_{K}^{\prime})^{-1}\mathbf{u}|_{K}\circ F_{K}\in\mathbf{RT}_{p}(\widehat{K})\},

(8.6b)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega)\,|\,(F_{K}^{\prime})^{T}\mathbf{u}|_{K}\circ F_{K}\in\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\widehat{K})\}.

(8.6c)

We set
8
8

8

Note that
\mathbf{X}_{h}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})
and
S_{h}=S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})
since

\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})\subset\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
and
S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})\subset H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
.

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}_{h}

\displaystyle:=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})\cap\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}},

\displaystyle S_{h}

\displaystyle:=S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})\cap H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)

(8.7)

and recall the well-known exact sequence property

S_{h}\overset{\nabla}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{X}_{h}\overset{\operatorname*{curl}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{X}_{h}.

(8.8)

The
hp
-FEM Galerkin discretization for the electric Maxwell problem
(
2.42
) is given by:

\text{find }\mathbf{E}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}\text{\quad such that }A_{k}(\mathbf{E}_{h},\mathbf{v})=\left(\mathbf{j,v}\right)+\left(\mathbf{g}_{T},\mathbf{v}\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}\left(\Gamma\right)}\quad\forall\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}.

(8.9)

8.2
hp
-Approximation operators

We will use polynomial approximation operators that are constructed
elementwise, i.e., for an operator
\widehat{I}_{p}
on the reference element

\widehat{K}
, a global operator
I_{p}
is defined
by setting
(I_{p}u)|_{K}:=\widehat{I}_{p}(u\circ F_{K}))\circ F_{K}^{-1}
. If
\widehat{I}_{p}
maps into
{\mathcal{P}}_{p+1}(\widehat{K})
, we say

\widehat{I}_{p}

admits an element-by-element construction
, if the
operator
I_{p}
defined in this way maps into
S_{p+1}({\mathcal{T}}_{h})
.
Analogously, if
\widehat{I}_{p}
maps into
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\widehat{K})
, then we say that
\widehat{I}_{p}

admits an element-by-element construction
if the resulting operator

I_{p}
maps into
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\mathcal{T}_{h})
.

For scalar functions (or gradient fields), we have elemental approximation
operators with the optimal convergence in
L^{2}
and
H^{1}
:

Lemma 8.3

Let
\widehat{K}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
d\in\{2,3\}
, be the reference triangle or reference
tetrahedron and

\mathbb{R}\ni m\geq(d+1)/2
. Then, for every
p\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, there exists a linear
operator
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}:H^{m}(\widehat{K})\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{p+1}
that permits an element-by-element
construction
such that if
p\geq m-2

\begin{split}&\|u-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}u\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K})}+\frac{1}{p+1}\|u-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}u\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}+(p+1)^{-1/2}\|u-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}u\|_{L^{2}(\partial\widehat{K})}\\
&\qquad\mbox{}+(p+1)^{-3/2}\|u-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}u\|_{H^{1}(\partial\widehat{K})}\leq C{(p+1)^{-m}}|u|_{H^{m}(\widehat{K})}\end{split}

(8.10)

for a constant
C>0
that depends only on

m
,
d
, and the choice of reference triangle/tetrahedron.

For the case
d=3
, the condition on

m

can be relaxed to

m>d/2
.

Proof.
The operator
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}
may be taken as the operators
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},3d}_{p+1}
for
d=3
or
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},2d}_{p+1}
for
d=2
of
[
MR20
]
. The
volume estimates follow from
[
MR20
, Cor. 2.12]
for the case
d=3

and
[
MR20
, Thm. 2.13]
for the case
d=2
.
For the estimates on
\partial\widehat{K}
, one notices that the restriction
of
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},3d}_{p+1}
to a boundary face

\widehat{f}
is the operator
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},2d}_{p+1}

on that face and that the restriction of
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},2d}_{p+1}
to an edge of the reference triangle is the operator
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{grad},1d}_{p+1}
discussed in
[
MR20
, Lem. 4.1]
.

For
d=3
an operator
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}
with the stated approximation
properties is constructed in
[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
for the
case

m>d/2=3/2
. The statement about the approximation on
\partial\widehat{K}

follows by a more careful analysis of the proof of
[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
. For the reader’s convenience, the proof is reproduced
in
Theorem
B.5
.

The fact that
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}
in
Lemma
8.3
has the element-by-element
construction property means that an elementwise definition of the operator

\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}:H^{m}(\Omega)\rightarrow S_{p+1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})
by

(\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi)|_{K}=(\widehat{\Pi}_{p}(\varphi\circ F_{K}))\circ F_{K}^{-1}
maps indeed into
S_{p+1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})\subset H^{1}(\Omega)
.

In the following we always assume for the spatial dimension
d=3
. By scaling
arguments we get the following result:

Corollary 8.4

Let
d=3
. For
m\in\mathbb{N}_{>3/2}
and
p\geq m-2
the
operator
\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}:H^{m}(\Omega)\rightarrow S_{p+1}(\mathcal{T}_{h})
has following the approximation properties for all
K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}
:

\displaystyle\!\!\!\|\varphi-\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi\|_{L^{2}(K)}+\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\|\varphi-\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi\|_{H^{1}(K)}\leq C\left(\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\right)^{m}\|\varphi\|_{H^{m}(K)},

(8.11)

\displaystyle\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\|\varphi-\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi\|_{H^{1}(\partial K)}\leq C\left(\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\right)^{m-1/2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{m}(K)}.

(8.12)

In
[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.2]
approximation operators

\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}:{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\operatorname*{curl},\widehat{K})\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\widehat{K})
and
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},s}:{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\operatorname*{div},\widehat{K})\rightarrow\mathbf{RT}_{p}(\widehat{K})
on the reference tetrahedron

\widehat{K}
are defined with certain elementwise approximation properties.
Global versions of these operators
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}:\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega)\cap{\prod_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\operatorname*{curl},K)\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}_{p}^{\operatorname*{I}}(\mathcal{T}_{h})

and
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},s}:\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{div},\Omega\right)\mathbf{\cap}{\prod_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}}{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(K,\operatorname*{div},\widehat{K})\rightarrow\mathbf{RT}_{p}\left(\mathcal{T}_{h}\right)
are characterized by lifting the operators on the
reference element by (cf.
[
MS21a
, Def. 8.1]
)

\displaystyle(\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u})|_{K}\circ F_{K}:=(F_{K}^{\prime})^{-T}\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}((F_{K}^{\prime})^{\top}\mathbf{u}\circ F_{K}),

(8.13)

\displaystyle(\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},s}\mathbf{u})|_{K}:=(\operatorname*{det}(F_{K}^{\prime}))^{-1}F_{K}^{\prime}(\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},s}(\operatorname*{det}F_{K}^{\prime})(F_{K}^{\prime})^{-1}\mathbf{u}\circ F_{K}))\circ F_{K}^{-1}.

(8.14)

The approximation properties of
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}
are inferred
from those of
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}
given in

[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.2]
. We obtain:

Lemma 8.5

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{>3/2}
and
p\geq m-1
. Let

\widetilde{C}
,
B>0
. Then there are constants
C
,
\sigma>0
depending
only on
\widetilde{C}
,
B
,
m
, and the constants of
Assumption
8.1
such that the following holds for the
operator
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}:\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)\rightarrow{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{N}}}_{p}^{\operatorname{I}}(\mathcal{T}_{h})
and all
K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}
:

(i)

If
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(K)
then

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(K)}+\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(K)}\leq C\left(\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\right)^{m}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(K)},

(8.15)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial K)}\leq C\left(\frac{h_{K}}{p+1}\right)^{m-1/2}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(K)}.

(8.16)

(ii)

If
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{u}}(K),B,K)
for some
C_{\mathbf{u}}(K)>0
and if

h_{K}+|k|h_{K}/p\leq\widetilde{C}

(8.17)

then

\displaystyle h_{K}^{1/2}\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial{K})}+\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}({K})}+h_{K}\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}({K})}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq CC_{\mathbf{u}}(K)\left(\left(\frac{h_{K}}{h_{K}+\sigma}\right)^{p+1}+\left(\frac{|k|h_{K}}{\sigma p}\right)^{p+1}\right).

(8.18)

(iii)

If
\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{A}(C_{\mathbf{u}},B,\Omega)
for some
C_{\mathbf{u}}>0
and if
(
8.17
) holds, then

\|\mathbf{u}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{u}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}|k|\left(\left(\frac{h}{h+\sigma}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right).

Proof.
The result follows from modifications of the procedure in
[
MS21a
, Sec. 8.3]
. We recall the structure
F_{K}=R_{K}\circ A_{K}
of
the element maps by Assumption
8.1
. For
K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}
we define
\widetilde{K}:=A_{K}(K)
and the transformed
functions
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}:=(F_{K}^{\prime})^{\top}\mathbf{v}\circ F_{K}

on
\widehat{K}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}:=(R_{K}^{\prime})^{\top}\mathbf{v}\circ R_{K}
on
\widetilde{K}
. We note that
\widehat{\mathbf{v}}=(A_{K}^{\prime})^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\circ A_{K}
. By
Assumption
8.1
and the fact that
A_{K}
is affine, we have

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{j}(\widetilde{K})}\sim\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{j}({K})},\qquad

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial\widetilde{K})}\sim\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial{K})},

(8.19)

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}|_{\mathbf{H}^{j}(\widehat{K})}\sim h_{K}^{1+j-3/2}|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}|_{\mathbf{H}^{j}(\widetilde{K})},\quad

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial\widehat{K})}\sim h_{K}^{1-1}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial{\widetilde{K}})},

(8.20)

where the implied constant depends only on
j
and the constants of
Assumption
8.1
.

Proof of (
i
):
From
[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.2]
, we have for

p\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\widehat{K})}+\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\widehat{K})}\leq Cp^{-(m-1)}|\mathbf{u}|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\widehat{K})}.

(8.21)

This approximation result and the scaling argument expressed in
(
8.19
), (
8.20
) produce (
8.15
). The multiplicative trace inequality
\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial\widehat{K})}^{2}\leq C\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\widehat{K})}\|\widehat{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\widehat{K})}
applied to (
8.21
)
and similar scaling arguments produce (
8.16
).

Proof of (
ii
):
By
[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.4]
, the pull-back
\widehat{\mathbf{u}}\in\mathcal{A}(CC_{\mathbf{u}}(K)h_{K}^{1-3/2},h_{K}B^{\prime},\widehat{K})
for
some
B^{\prime}
depending only on
B
and the constants of
Assumption
8.1
. By
[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.2]

there are constants depending only on
B
and the constants of
Assumption
8.1
such that

\|\widehat{\mathbf{u}}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\widehat{\mathbf{u}}\|_{W^{2,\infty}(\widehat{K})}\leq Ch_{K}^{1-3/2}C_{\mathbf{u}}(K)\left(\left(\frac{h_{K}}{h_{K}+\sigma}\right)^{p+1}+\left(\frac{|k|h_{K}}{\sigma p}\right)^{p+1}\right).

With similar scaling arguments as in the proof of (
i
), we obtain the stated estimate.

Proof of (
iii
):
For each
K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}
, we define

\widetilde{C}_{\mathbf{u}}^{2}(K):=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{|\mathbf{u}|_{\mathbf{H}^{n}(K)}^{2}}{(2B)^{2n}\max(n+1,|k|)^{2n}}

and note

\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{A}(\widetilde{C}_{\mathbf{u}}(K),2B,K)\qquad\mbox{with}\qquad\sum_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}\widetilde{C}_{\mathbf{u}}^{2}(K)\leq 2C_{\mathbf{u}}^{2}.

We then sum the elementwise error estimates provided by
(
ii
).

8.3
An interpolating projector on the finite element space

For the error analysis, the following subspaces of
\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)

will play an important role:

\mathbf{V}_{k,0}:=\left\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}\mid{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{u},\nabla\varphi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}=0\quad\forall\varphi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)\right\}.

(8.22)

Proposition 8.6

Let
\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with simply
connected, analytic boundary. The space
\mathbf{V}_{k,0}
can alternatively be characterized by

\mathbf{V}_{k,0}=\left\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}\mid\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{u}=0\wedge\operatorname*{i}k\left\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{n}\right\rangle+\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{u}_{T}=0\quad\text{on }\Gamma\right\}.

(8.23)

The proof of this proposition is standard and uses the same arguments as,
e.g.,
[
MS21a
, Lem. 4.10]
.

Proposition 8.7

Let
\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with simply
connected analytic boundary. It holds
\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{k,0}\subset\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)
, and there exists
c>0
independent of
k
such that

c\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right),k}\leq\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}\leq\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\qquad\forall\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{V}_{k,0}.

Proof.
The estimate
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),k}\leq\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}
follows directly from the
definition of the norms. For the lower bound, we employ the Helmholtz
decomposition of
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{V}_{k,0}

as in Lemma
2.7
(
i
)
and take into account
\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{v}=0
. That is, there
exist
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)
and
\varphi\in H^{1}(\Omega)

with

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=\nabla\varphi+\mathbf{w},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{v}\right\|.

(8.24)

Since
\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{v}=0
we conclude
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{div},\Omega)}=\|\mathbf{v}\|
so that a trace theorem gives us

\|\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}\rangle\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{div},\Omega)}=C\|\mathbf{v}\|.

It holds

\Delta_{\Gamma}(\varphi|_{\Gamma})=\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{v}_{T}-\mathbf{w}_{T})\overset{\text{Prop.~\ref{PropV0incl}}}{=}-\operatorname*{i}k\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}\rangle-\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{T}=:\tilde{v}\text{.}

By the smoothness of the closed manifold
\Gamma
and the shift
properties of the Laplace-Beltrami operator we get

\displaystyle\|\varphi\|_{H^{3/2}(\Gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C\left[\|\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}\mathbf{w}_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}\left(\Gamma\right)}+\left|k\right|\|\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{n}\rangle\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\|\mathbf{w}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}+|k|\|\mathbf{v}\|\right]\leq C\left(\left\|\mathbf{w}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)}+\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(\ref{wcurlv})}}{\leq}C\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}.

Since
\varphi
solves

-\Delta\varphi=\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{w}\text{{\hskip 11.49994pt}in }\Omega,

the shift theorem for the Laplace operator on smooth domains leads to

\|\nabla\varphi\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq C\!\left(\|\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{w}\|+\|\varphi\|_{H^{3/2}(\Gamma)}\right)\leq C\!\left(\|\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{v}\|+\|\mathbf{v}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega),k}\right).

(8.25)

The combination of (
8.24
) and (
8.25
) shows that

\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)
and

\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega\right)}\leq C\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}\leq C\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

Since we have trivially
\left|k\right|\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|\leq\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}
, the assertion follows.

We also need the following subspace of
\mathbf{V}_{k,0}

given by

{\mathbf{V}}_{k,0,h}:=\left\{{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{V}}_{k,0}\mid\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{v}}\in\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{X}}_{h}\right\}.

(8.26)

The operator
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}
in (
8.13
),
(
8.14
) has (
p
-optimal) approximation properties in
\|\cdot\|_{\operatorname*{curl},\Omega,k}
as it has simultaneously
p
-optimal
approximation properties in
L^{2}
and
H^{1}
. However, it is not a
projection and does not have the commuting diagram property. Since this is
needed for the estimate of the consistency term in Section
9.2
we
employ operators,
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},c}
,
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},c}
, which enjoy these properties. They were constructed
in
[
MR20
]
in an element-by-element fashion and used in

[
MS21a
, Thm. 8.2]
. The choice
\Pi_{h}^{E}:\mathbf{V}_{k,0,h}+\mathbf{X}_{h}\rightarrow\mathbf{X}_{h}
as
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},c}
and the companion operator
\Pi_{h}^{F}:H(\operatorname{div},\Omega)\cap\prod_{K\in{\mathcal{T}}_{h}}{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\operatorname{div},K)\rightarrow\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{X}_{h}
as

\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},c}
allows us to derive quantitative convergence
estimates in Section
9
.

Lemma 8.8

The operators
\Pi_{h}^{E}:=\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},c}
and
\Pi_{h}^{F}:=\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{div},c}
satisfy the following properties:

\Pi_{h}^{E}:\mathbf{V}_{k,0,h}+\mathbf{X}_{h}\rightarrow{\mathbf{X}}_{h}
and

\Pi_{h}^{F}:H(\operatorname{div},\Omega)\cap\prod_{K\in{\mathcal{T}}_{h}}{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\operatorname{div},K)\rightarrow\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{X}_{h}
are linear mappings with

(i)

\Pi_{h}^{E}
is a projection, i.e., the
restriction
\left.\Pi_{h}^{E}\right|_{\mathbf{X}_{h}}
is the identity
on
\mathbf{X}_{h}
.

(ii)

The operators
\Pi_{h}^{E}
have the commuting
property:
\operatorname*{curl}\Pi_{h}^{E}=\Pi_{h}^{F}\operatorname*{curl}
.

Proof.
Since
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},c}
is based on an element-by-element
construction it is well defined on
\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega)\cap{\prod_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}}\mathbf{H}^{1}(\operatorname*{curl},K)
. Since
\mathbf{V}_{k,0,h}+\mathbf{X}_{h}
is a subspace of this space,
the mapping properties follow.
The projection property of
\Pi_{h}^{E}
and the commuting property of

\Pi_{h}^{E}
and
\Pi_{h}^{F}
are proved in
[
MR20
, Thm. 2.10, Rem. 2.11]
.

9
Stability and convergence of the Galerkin discretization

The wavenumber-explicit stability and convergence analysis for Maxwell’s
equations with transparent boundary conditions has been developed recently in

[
MS21a
]
and generalizes the theory in
[
Mon03
, Sec. 7.2]
. A “roadmap” for the convergence proof of
[
MS21a
]

is given in
[
MS21a
, Sec. 1.1–1.3]
. In the present analysis,
we follow this “roadmap” taking into account the change in boundary conditions
from transparent boundary conditions to impedance boundary conditions.
A key role is played by the term

{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}=A_{k}(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})-\left(\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u,}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{v}\right)
from
(
2.45
), which includes the boundary conditions. This sesquilinear form
determines the space
\mathbf{V}_{k,0}
(see (
8.22
)) and the regular decomposition
in Def.
9.2
ahead and its properties differentiate the present case of
impedance boundary conditions from the transparent boundary condition case.
Compared to the case of transparent boundary conditions, the present impedance boundary conditions
case is simpler in that fewer approximation quantities
\eta^{\operatorname{alg}}_{j}
,
\tilde{\eta}_{j}^{\operatorname{alg}}

are required in the analysis.

In this section, we develop a stability and convergence theory for
Maxwell’s equations with impedance boundary conditions, see
Sect.
2.5
. Recall the definition of the sesquilinear form

{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\cdot,\cdot\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}
of (
2.45
) and of the norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{k,+}
in Definition
2.5
.

We introduce the quantity
\delta_{k}:\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by
\delta_{k}\left(\mathbf{0}\right):=0\
and for

\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}
by

\delta_{k}(\mathbf{w}):=\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}}\left(2\frac{|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{w},\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}|}{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\|\mathbf{v}_{h}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}\right),

(9.1)

which will play the important role of a consistency term.

Proposition 9.1
(quasi-optimality)

Let
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
and
\mathbf{E}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}
satisfy

A_{k}(\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{E}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h})=0\qquad\forall\,\mathbf{v}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}.

Assume that
\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})<1
for
\mathbf{e}_{h}:=\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{E}_{h}
. Then,
\mathbf{e}_{h}
satisfies, for all

\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}
, the quasi-optimal error estimate

\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\leq\frac{1+\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})}{1-\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})}\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

Proof.
The definitions of the sesquilinear forms
A_{k}
and
{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\cdot,\cdot\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}
imply

\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{{}_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}^{2}=\left|A_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{e}_{h})+2{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{e}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|.

(9.2)

We employ Galerkin orthogonality for the first term in
(
9.2
) to obtain for any
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}

\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}^{2}\leq\bigl|A_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h})+2{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\bigr|+\delta_{k}\left({\mathbf{e}}_{h}\right)\bigl\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\!\!\!\!\underset{\leq\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}+\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}{\underbrace{\left\|\mathbf{E}_{h}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}}.

We write
A_{k}
in the form (
2.46
) so that

\displaystyle\left(1-\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})\right)\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left(\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{e}_{h},\operatorname{curl}\left(\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right)\right)+{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|

(9.3)

\displaystyle\quad+\delta_{k}({\mathbf{e}}_{h})\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

The sesquilinear form
{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\cdot,\cdot\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}
is continuous, and we have

\bigl|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\bigr|\leq\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{k,+}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{k,+}\qquad\forall\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}.

(9.4)

Hence,

\displaystyle\left(1-\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})\right)\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

\displaystyle\qquad\mbox{}+\delta_{k}({\mathbf{e}}_{h})\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k},

and the assertion follows.

9.1
Splitting of the consistency term

We introduce continuous and discrete Helmholtz decompositions that are adapted
to the problem under consideration.

Definition 9.2

On
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
the

Helmholtz splittings

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}+\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v},

(9.5a)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}+\Pi_{k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}

(9.5b)

are given via operators
\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}
,
\Pi_{k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}

and their discrete counterparts
\Pi_{k,h}^{\nabla}
,
\Pi_{k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}
by seeking
\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\in\nabla H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega)
and
\Pi_{k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\in\nabla S_{h}
such that

\displaystyle{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v},\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{v},\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\qquad\forall\psi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega),

(9.6a)

\displaystyle{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\Pi_{k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v},\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{v},\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\qquad\forall\psi\in S_{h}.

(9.6b)

The operators
\Pi_{k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}
,
\Pi_{k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}
are then given via the relations
(
9.5
).

It is easy to see (cf. (
2.48
)) that

\displaystyle{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi,\Pi_{-k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi,\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\qquad\forall\psi\in H_{\operatorname*{imp}}^{1}(\Omega),

(9.7a)

\displaystyle{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi,\Pi_{-k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi,\mathbf{v}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\qquad\forall\psi\in S_{h}.

(9.7b)

Solvability of these equations follows trivially from the Lax-Milgram lemma as
can be seen from the following lemma.

Lemma 9.3

Problems (
9.6
) have unique solutions,
which satisfy

\displaystyle\bigl\|\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}+\bigr\|\Pi_{k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl\|\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k},

\displaystyle\bigl\|\Pi_{k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}+\bigl\|\Pi_{k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigl\|\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

Proof.
We first consider the continuous problem (
9.6a
). Taking

\sigma=\exp\left(\left(\operatorname*{sign}k\right)\operatorname*{i}\frac{\pi}{4}\right)
we obtain coercivity as in the proof of
Theorem
4.3
(
i
) via

\operatorname{Re}{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi,\sigma\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}=2^{-1/2}\!\left[k^{2}\left(\nabla\psi,\nabla\psi\right)+\left|k\right|\left(\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi,\nabla_{\Gamma}\psi\right)_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\right]\!=2^{-1/2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}^{2}.

The continuity follows from (
9.4
):

\left|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\varphi,\nabla\psi\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|\leq\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{k,+}\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{k,+}=\left\|\nabla\varphi\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.8)

This implies existence, uniqueness, and the
a priori
estimate

\bigl\|\Pi_{k}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\leq\sqrt{2}\left\|\mathbf{v}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

The estimate of
\Pi_{k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}
follows by a
triangle inequality. Since the coercivity and continuity estimates are
inherited by the finite dimensional subspace
\nabla S_{h}
, well-posedness
also follows on the discrete level. The estimates for the other operators
follow verbatim.

The principal splitting of the consistency term
\delta_{k}
in
(
9.1
) is introduced next. We write

\displaystyle{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}=

(9.9)

\displaystyle\quad\underset{=:T_{1}}{\underbrace{{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\left(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\right)\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}}}+\underset{=:T_{2}}{\underbrace{{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}}}+\underset{=:T_{3}}{\underbrace{{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\Pi_{-k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}}}.

Galerkin orthogonality implies
{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\Pi_{-k,h}^{\nabla}\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}=0
, i.e.,
T_{3}=0
.

9.2
Consistency analysis: the term
\mathbf{T}_{1}
in
(
9.9
)

The continuity of the sesquilinear form
{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\cdot,\cdot\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}
(cf. (
9.4
)) implies

\left|T_{1}\right|\leq\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{k,+}\bigl\|\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}.

(9.10)

The definition of the discrete and continuous Helmholtz decomposition applied
to a discrete function
\mathbf{v}_{h}
leads to (cf. Def.
9.2
, (
9.7
))

{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\nabla\psi_{h},\left(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\right)\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}=0\quad\forall\psi_{h}\in S_{h}.

(9.11)

We use (
9.5
) to get
\operatorname*{curl}\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}=\operatorname*{curl}\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}=\operatorname*{curl}
on
\mathbf{X}_{h}
and thus

\displaystyle\operatorname*{curl}\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\bigr)\overset{\text{Lem.~\ref{AdiscSp}(\ref{item:AdiscSp-b}) }}{=}

(9.12)

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname*{curl}\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\bigr)-\Pi_{h}^{F}\operatorname*{curl}\bigl(\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\bigr)=\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}-\Pi_{h}^{F}\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\overset{\text{Lem.~\ref{AdiscSp}(\ref{item:AdiscSp-b})}}{=}

\displaystyle\quad\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}-\operatorname*{curl}\Pi_{h}^{E}{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\overset{\text{Lem.~\ref{AdiscSp}(\ref{item:AdiscSp-a}) }}{=}\operatorname*{curl}\left({\mathbf{v}}_{h}-{\mathbf{v}}_{h}\right)=0.

By the exact sequence property (
8.8
), the observation
(
9.12
) implies that
\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}=\nabla\psi_{h}
for some
\psi_{h}\in S_{h}
and therefore

\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}=\nabla\psi_{h}+\bigl((\Pi_{h}^{E}-I)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}.

(9.13)

For the second factor in (
9.10
) we get by the Galerkin orthogonality
(
9.11
) and (
9.13
)

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\right)\mathbf{v}_{h}\right\|_{k,+}^{2}\!\!

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Re}{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\bigl(\Pi_{h}^{E}-I)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h},(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle+\left(\operatorname*{sign}k\right)\operatorname{Im}{\left(\kern-2.5pt\left(\kern-0.50003pt\ \bigl(\Pi_{h}^{E}-I\bigr)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h},\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\kern-2.5pt\right)_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\bigl\|\bigl(\Pi_{h}^{E}-I\bigr)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}\bigl\|\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}

so that

\bigl\|\bigl(\Pi_{-k,h}^{\operatorname*{curl}}-\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\bigr)\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}\leq 2\bigr\|\bigl(\Pi_{h}^{E}-I\bigr)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}.

This leads to the estimate of
T_{1}

\left|T_{1}\right|\leq 2\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{k,+}\bigl\|\bigl(I-\Pi_{h}^{E}\bigr)\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{k,+}.

We set
9
9

9

Our choice of notation is motivated by the appearance of similar approximation
quantities in the companion paper
[
MS21a
]
(for transparent boundary
conditions), where various measures of approximability
\eta_{j}^{\exp}
,

j\in\left\{1,3,4,5,7\right\}
, and
\eta_{j}^{\operatorname*{alg}}
,

j\in\left\{2,6\right\}
, were introduced and used in the
convergence analysis. Here, only the quantities
\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}
in (
9.14
) and
\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}
in
(
9.24
) are needed.

\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}:=\eta_{6}^{\operatorname{alg}}\bigl(\mathbf{X}_{h},\Pi_{h}^{E}\bigr):=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}\colon\\
\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{w}}\in\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{X}}_{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{\bigl\|\mathbf{w}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\mathbf{w}\bigr\|_{k,+}}{\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}}

(9.14)

and obtain

\displaystyle\left|T_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\|_{k,+}\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\bigl\|\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq 2C\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{k,+}\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\bigl\|\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}\bigr\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{C}\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{k,+}\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left\|\mathbf{v}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.15)

9.2.1
hp
-Analysis of
T_{1}

In
[
MS21a
, (4.72)]
it was proved that for our choice

\Pi_{h}^{E}:=\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},c}
with
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},c}
as in
[
MR20
]
,
[
MS21a
, §8]
(see Lem.
8.8
), one has

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}\colon\\
\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{w}}\in\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{X}}_{h}\end{subarray}}\frac{|k|\bigl\|\mathbf{w}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\mathbf{w}\bigr\|}{\left\|\mathbf{w}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}}\leq C\frac{\left|k\right|h}{p}.

(9.16)

For the boundary term in the norm
\|\cdot\|_{k,+}
we study the
approximation properties of the operator
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},c}
of

[
MR20
]
on the boundary of the reference tetrahedron

\widehat{K}
more carefully.

Lemma 9.4

Let
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},3d}
be the operator introduced in

[
MR20
]
. For all
\mathbf{u}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\widehat{K})
with
\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{u}\in(\mathcal{P}_{p}(\widehat{K}))^{3}
, there holds with the
tangential component operator
\Pi_{T,\partial\widehat{K}}

\bigl\|\Pi_{T,\partial\widehat{K}}\bigl(\mathbf{u}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},3d}\mathbf{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\partial\widehat{K})}\leq Cp^{-1/2}\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}.

Proof.
We follow the proof of
[
MR20
, Lem. 6.15]
and employ the
notation used there. From
[
MR20
, proof of Lem. 6.15]
and

[
MR20
, (6.42)]
, we can decompose
{\mathbf{u}}=\nabla\varphi+{\mathbf{v}}
with

\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\widehat{K})}+\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\widehat{K})}\leq C\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(\widehat{K})}.

(9.17)

Since
\operatorname{curl}\mathbf{u}\in(\mathcal{P}_{p}(\widehat{K}))^{3}
we have
\mathbf{v}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},3d}\mathbf{v}=0
. We conclude

\mathbf{u}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},3d}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{v}+\nabla\varphi-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},3d}(\mathbf{v}+\nabla\varphi)=\nabla(\varphi-\widehat{\Pi}_{p+1}^{\operatorname*{grad},3d}\varphi)

with
\widehat{\Pi}_{p+1}^{\operatorname*{grad},3d}
as in

[
MR20
]
. The construction of the projection-based
interpolation operators
\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},3d}
,

\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname{grad},3d}
is such that facewise, they
reduce to corresponding 2D operators. That is, for each face
f\subset\partial\widehat{K}
we have

\Pi_{T,\partial\widehat{K}}\bigl(\mathbf{u}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},3d}\mathbf{u}\bigr)|_{f}=\Pi_{T,\partial\widehat{K}}\bigl(\nabla\bigl(\varphi-\widehat{\Pi}_{p+1}^{\operatorname*{grad},3d}\varphi\bigr)\bigr)|_{f}=\nabla_{{f}}\bigl(\mathbf{I}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p+1}^{\operatorname*{grad},2d}\bigr)\bigl(\varphi|_{f}\bigr).

We apply
[
MR20
, Thm. 2.13]
to obtain

\displaystyle\bigl\|\Pi_{T,\partial\widehat{K}}\bigl(\mathbf{u}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},3d}\mathbf{u}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(f)}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|\nabla_{{f}}\bigl(\mathbf{I}-\widehat{\Pi}_{p+1}^{\operatorname*{grad},2d}\bigr)\varphi|_{f}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(f)}\leq Cp^{-1/2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{3/2}(f)}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-1/2}\|\varphi\|_{H^{2}(\widehat{K})}\overset{\text{(\ref{StabRojikDecomp})}}{\leq}Cp^{-1/2}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}.

For the boundary part of
\left\|\mathbf{w}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}
of a
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}
with

\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{w}}\in\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{X}}_{h}
, we
get, by applying a scaling argument to Lemma
9.4
:

\displaystyle|k|\bigl\|\Pi_{T}\bigl(\mathbf{w}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\mathbf{w}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}^{2}

\displaystyle=|k|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}\\
\left|K\cap\Gamma\right|>0\end{subarray}}\bigl\|\Pi_{T}\bigl(\mathbf{w}-\Pi_{h}^{E}\mathbf{w}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(K\cap\Gamma)}^{2}

(9.18)

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{|k|h}{p}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}\\
\left|K\cap\Gamma\right|>0\end{subarray}}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(K)}^{2}\leq C\frac{|k|h}{p}\|\mathbf{w}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}.

The combination of (
9.16
) with (
9.18
) leads to

\eta_{6}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\leq C\left(\frac{|k|h}{p}\right)^{1/2}\left(1+\left(\frac{\left|k\right|h}{p}\right)^{1/2}\right).

(9.19)

9.3
Consistency analysis: the term
\mathbf{T}_{2}
in
(
9.9
)

Recall the definition of
T_{2}={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{0}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}
with
\mathbf{v}_{0}:=\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}=(\operatorname{I}-\Pi_{-k}^{\nabla})\mathbf{v}_{h}
. The function
\mathbf{v}_{0}
belongs to
\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
and by combining (
9.5a
) and
(
9.6a
) we find that
\mathbf{v}_{0}
belongs to
\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}
. Proposition
8.7
implies
\mathbf{v}_{0}\in\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)
and

\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}\leq|k|\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega),k}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega),k}\leq C\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.20)

The characterization (
8.23
) of
\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}
implies

\operatorname*{div}\nolimits_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}=\operatorname*{i}k\langle\mathbf{v}_{0},\mathbf{n}\rangle.

(9.21)

To estimate the term
T_{2}
, we consider the dual problem: Given

\mathbf{v}_{0}\in\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}
, find
\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
such that

A_{k}(\mathbf{w},\mathbf{z})={\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{w},\mathbf{v}_{0}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\quad\forall\mathbf{w}\in\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}.

The operator
\mathcal{N}_{-k}:\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}\rightarrow\mathbf{X}_{\operatorname*{imp}}
is defined by
\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}:=\mathbf{z}
. The strong formulation is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Omega,-k}\mathbf{z}

\displaystyle=k^{2}\mathbf{v}_{0}\quad\text{in }\Omega,

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\Gamma,-k}\mathbf{z}

\displaystyle=-\operatorname*{i}k\left(\mathbf{v}_{0}\right)_{T}\quad\text{on }\Gamma.

(9.22)

Hence,
\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}=\mathcal{S}_{\Omega,-k}^{\operatorname*{MW}}\left(k^{2}\mathbf{v}_{0},-\operatorname*{i}k\left(\mathbf{v}_{0}\right)_{T}\right)
. By Galerkin orthogonality satisfied by

\mathbf{e}_{h}
, we have for any
\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}

\left|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{0}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|=\left|A_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}-\mathbf{w}_{h})\right|\leq\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.23)

We set

\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left(\mathbf{X}_{h}\right):=\sup_{\mathbf{v}_{0}\in\mathbf{V}_{-k,0}\backslash\left\{\mathbf{0}\right\}}\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}}\frac{\left\|\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}{\left\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}}

(9.24)

so that

\left|T_{2}\right|=\left|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{0}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|\leq\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left(\mathbf{X}_{h}\right)\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.25)

9.3.1
hp
-Analysis of
T_{2}

Next, we gauge the approximation property
\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left(\mathbf{X}_{h}\right)
. We employ the
splitting given by Theorem
7.3
, viz.,

\mathcal{N}_{-k}\mathbf{v}_{0}=\mathbf{z}=\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}+\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}+k^{-2}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}-\operatorname{i}k^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}.

(9.26)

Note that these
five
functions
\mathbf{z}
,
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}
,
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}
,

\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}
,
\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
depend on
\mathbf{v}_{0}
but
we suppress this in the notation. From Theorem
7.3
with

m=m^{\prime}=1
we have

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|k\right|^{2}\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\overset{\operatorname{div}\mathbf{v}_{0}=0}{=}0,

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\leq C|k|\|(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}\|_{H^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\overset{(\ref{v0estH1})}{\leq}C|k|\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq\left|k\right|^{2}\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega),k}\overset{\text{(\ref{zH2estimate0})}}{\leq}C|k|^{-1}\left(\|k^{2}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}(\Omega)}+|k|\|k(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1/2}(\Gamma)}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{v0estH1})}{\leq}C|k|\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k},

\displaystyle\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{A}(CC_{\mathbf{z}},B,\Omega),

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=|k|^{\theta-1}\bigl(\|k^{2}\mathbf{v}_{0}\|+|k|\|k(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\overset{\operatorname{i}k(\mathbf{v}_{0})_{T}=\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{v}_{0},(\ref{v0estH1})}{\leq}C|k|^{\theta}\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}.

(9.27)

We note that
\varphi_{\mathbf{f}}=0
. For the approximation of
\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}
, we use the elementwise defined operator
\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}
of Corollary
8.4
with
m=2
there to get

\displaystyle\bigl\|k^{-1}\bigl(\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}-\nabla\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\bigr)\bigr\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|^{-1}\left(|k|\frac{h}{p}+|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{1/2}\right)\|\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{|k|h}{p}\right)^{1/2}\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}.

(9.28)

For the approximation of
\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}
, we employ the elementwise
defined operator
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}:\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathbf{X}_{h}
as in Lemma
8.5
. By summing
over all elements the estimates of Lemma
8.5
(
i
) we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}\left(\operatorname*{curl},\Omega\right),k}^{2}=\sum_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname*{curl},K),k}^{2}

(9.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\sum_{K\in\mathcal{T}_{h}}\frac{h_{K}^{2}}{p^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\left|k\right|^{2}h_{K}^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(K)}^{2}\leq C\frac{h^{2}}{p^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\left|k\right|^{2}h^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\leq C\frac{\left|k\right|^{2}h^{2}}{p^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\left|k\right|^{2}h^{2}}{p^{2}}\right)\left\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}^{2}.

For the boundary part of the
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}
norm we proceed similarly using
Lemma
8.5
(
i
) to arrive at

|k|^{1/2}\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Gamma)}\leq\!C|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{3/2}\!\!\!\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{2}(\Omega)}\leq\!C\!\left(\frac{|k|h}{p}\right)^{3/2}\!\!\!\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}.

In summary, we have proved

\|\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname*{curl},s}\mathbf{z}_{H^{2}}\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\leq C\frac{|k|h}{p}\left(1+\left(\frac{h\left|k\right|}{p}\right)^{1/2}+\frac{h\left|k\right|}{p}\right)\left\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.30)

Next, for the analytic part
\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}
we get from
Lemma
8.5
(
iii
) in view of
(
9.27
) under the (mild) resolution condition

h+\frac{\left|k\right|h}{p}\leq\widetilde{C}

(9.31)

that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{z}_{\mathcal{A}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{z}}\left|k\right|\left(\left(\frac{h}{h+\sigma}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right)

(9.32)

\displaystyle\leq C\left\|\mathbf{v}_{0}\right\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}|k|^{\theta+1}\left(\left(\frac{h}{h+\sigma}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right).

This derivation is summarized in the following lemma.

Lemma 9.5

Assume hypothesis (
3.2
) and let

\Omega
be a bounded Lipschitz domain with simply connected, analytic
boundary. Let the mesh satisfy
Assumption
8.1
. Let
c_{2}
,
\varepsilon>0
be given. Then
there exists
c_{1}>0
(depending only on the constants of
(
3.2
),
\Omega
, the parameters of
Assumption
8.1
, and
c_{2}
,
\varepsilon
) such that for

h
,
k
,
p
satisfying the resolution condition

\frac{\left|k\right|h}{p}\leq c_{1}\quad\text{and\hskip 10.22217pt}p\geq\max\left\{1,c_{2}\ln\left|k\right|\right\}

(9.33)

there holds

\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left(\mathbf{X}_{h}\right)\leq\varepsilon.

(9.34)

Proof.
We combine (
9.28
), (
9.30
), and
(
9.32
) with the resolution condition to arrive at

\tilde{\eta}_{2}^{\operatorname*{alg}}\left(\mathbf{X}_{h}\right)\leq C\left(\left(\frac{h\left|k\right|}{p}\right)^{1/2}+{\left|k\right|^{\theta+1}}\left[\left(\frac{h}{\sigma+h}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{\left|k\right|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right]\right).

(9.35)

Clearly, by selecting
c_{1}
sufficiently small, we may ensure
that the first term in (
9.35
),
(|k|h/p)^{1/2}
, is smaller than
\varepsilon/3
.
The second term in (
9.35
),
|k|^{\theta+1}(h/(\sigma+h))^{p}
, can be made smaller than
\varepsilon/3

for sufficiently small
c_{1}
by appealing to
[
MS21a
, Lem. 8.7]
.
For the last term in (
9.35
), we may assume that
c_{1}<\sigma
and then estimate

\displaystyle|k|^{\theta+1}\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq|k|^{\theta+1}(c_{1}/\sigma)^{p}\leq|k|^{\theta+1}(c_{1}/\sigma)^{\max\{1,c_{2}\ln|k|\}}

\displaystyle=\min\{|k|^{\theta+1}(c_{1}/\sigma),|k|^{\theta+1+c_{2}\ln(c_{1}/\sigma)}\}.

This expression can be made smaller than
\varepsilon/3
uniformly in
|k|\in[1,\infty)
by selecting
c_{1}
sufficiently small:
the first term in the minimum tends to
0
as
c_{1}\rightarrow 0
uniformly in
|k|\in[1,2]
and the second term in the minimum
tends to zero as
c_{1}\rightarrow 0
uniformly in
|k|\geq 2
.

9.4
h
-
p
-
k
-explicit stability and convergence estimates for
Maxwell’s equations

We begin with the estimate of the consistency term
\delta_{k}
.

Lemma 9.6

Let the assumptions in Lemma
9.5
hold. Let

\varepsilon
,
c_{2}>0
be given. Then, one can choose a constant
c_{1}>0

sufficiently small such that the resolution condition (
9.33
) implies

\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})\leq\varepsilon.

Proof.
We
combine estimates (
9.8
), (
9.9
),
(
9.15
), (
9.19
), (
9.25
)
for
\mathbf{v}_{0}:=\Pi_{-k}^{\operatorname*{curl}}\mathbf{v}_{h}
,
Lemma
9.3
, and (
9.34
) in a straightforward way to obtain

\left|{\left(\!\left(\kern-0.50003pt\mathbf{e}_{h},\mathbf{v}_{h}\kern-1.00006pt\right)\!\right)}_{k}\right|\leq C\bigl(c_{1}^{1/2}+c_{1}+\varepsilon\bigr)\left\|\mathbf{e}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\left\|\mathbf{v}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}

and thus
\displaystyle\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})\leq 2C(c_{1}^{1/2}+c_{1}+\varepsilon).
We may assume that
\sqrt{c_{1}}\leq\varepsilon\leq 1
so
that
\displaystyle\delta_{k}(\mathbf{e}_{h})\leq 6C\varepsilon
. Since the
constant
C>0
does not depend on
\varepsilon
, the result follows by
adjusting constants.

This estimate allows us to formulate the quasi-optimality of the
hp
-FEM
Galerkin discretization and to show
h
-
p
-
k
-explicit convergence rates
under suitable regularity assumptions.

Theorem 9.7

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
be a bounded
Lipschitz domain with a simply connected, analytic boundary. Let the stability
Assumption
3.2
be satisfied.
Let the finite element mesh with mesh size
h
satisfy
Assumption
8.1
, and let
\mathbf{X}_{h}
be defined by as
the space of Nédélec-type-I elements of degree
p
(cf. (
8.7
)).

Then, for any
\mathbf{j}
,
\mathbf{g}_{T}
satisfying
(
2.41
), the variational form of Maxwell’s equations
(
2.42
) has a unique solution
\mathbf{E}
.

For any fixed
c_{2}>0
and
\eta\in(0,1)
one can select
c_{1}>0

(depending only on
\Omega
and the constants of (
3.2
)
and Assumption
8.1
) such that the resolution condition
(
9.33
) implies that the discrete problem (
8.9
) has a unique
solution
\mathbf{E}_{h}
, which satisfies the quasi-optimal error estimate

\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{E}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}\leq\frac{1+\eta}{1-\eta}\inf_{\mathbf{w}_{h}\in\mathbf{X}_{h}}\left\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{w}_{h}\right\|_{\operatorname*{imp},k}.

(9.36)

Proof.
Existence and uniqueness of the continuous variational Maxwell problem follow
from Proposition
3.1
. From
Lemma
9.6
we know that
c_{1}
can be chosen sufficiently small
such that
\delta(\mathbf{e}_{h})<\eta
. As in the proof of Theorem

[
MS21a
, Thm. 4.15]
(which goes back to
[
LM11
, Thm. 3.9]
) existence, uniqueness, and quasi-optimality follows.

The quasi-optimality result (
9.36
) leads to quantitative,

k
-explicit error estimates if a
k
-explicit regularity of the solution

\mathbf{E}
is available. In the following corollary, we draw on the
regularity assertions of Theorem
7.3
. We point out, however, that
due to our relying on the operator
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}
and the
regularity assertion Theorem
7.3
, the regularity requirements on
the data
\mathbf{j}
,
\mathbf{g}_{T}
are not the weakest possible ones.

Corollary 9.8

Let the hypotheses of
Theorem
9.7
be valid. Given
\eta\in(0,1)
and

c_{2}>0
let
c_{1}
be as in Theorem
9.7
. Then, under
the scale resolution condition (
9.33
) the following holds: Let
m
,

m^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
(\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g}_{T})\in\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)
together with

(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{j},\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T})\in\mathbf{H}^{m^{\prime}}(\Omega)\times\mathbf{H}^{m^{\prime}-1/2}(\Gamma)
.
If
p\geq\max(m,m^{\prime})
, then

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}-\mathbf{E}_{h}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{1+\eta}{1-\eta}\Biggl\{C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m}+C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}}|k|^{-3/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m^{\prime}+1/2}

(9.37)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\mbox{}+|k|C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},\mathcal{A}}\left(\left(\frac{h}{h+\sigma}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right)\Biggr\},

where

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m}

\displaystyle:=|k|^{-1}\|\mathbf{j}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m}(\Omega)}+\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m-1/2}(\Gamma)},

(9.38)

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{j}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m^{\prime}}(\Omega)}+|k|\|\operatorname{div}_{\Gamma}\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m^{\prime}-1/2}(\Gamma)},

(9.39)

\displaystyle C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle:=\left|k\right|^{\theta-1}\left(\|\mathbf{j}\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+|k|\|\mathbf{g}_{T}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{-1/2}(\Gamma)}\right).

(9.40)

Proof.
For the error estimate (
9.37
), we employ the solution
decomposition provided by Thm.
7.3
:

\mathbf{E}=\mathbf{E}_{H^{2}}+\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{A}}+k^{-2}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}+\operatorname{i}k^{-1}\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}

with

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}_{H^{2}}\|_{\mathbf{H}^{m+1}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m},

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}\|_{H^{m^{\prime}+2}(\Omega)}+|k|\|\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\|_{H^{m^{\prime}+2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{A}}

\displaystyle\in\mathcal{A}(CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},\mathcal{A}},B,\Omega)

for
k
-independent constants
C
,
B
. With the operators
\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,p}
and
\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}
of Corollary
8.4
and
Lemma
8.5
we get
