\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}_{H^{2}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{E}_{H^{2}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m}\!\left[\!\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m}\!\!+|k|\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m+1}\!\!+|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m+1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m},

\displaystyle\left|k\right|^{-2}\|\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}-\nabla\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi_{\mathbf{j}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}}|k|^{-2}\!\left[\!|k|\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m^{\prime}+1}+|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m^{\prime}+1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}}\left|k\right|^{-2}|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m^{\prime}+1/2},

\displaystyle|k|^{-1}\|\nabla\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}-\nabla\Pi_{p}^{\nabla,s}\varphi_{\mathbf{g}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},m^{\prime}}\left|k\right|^{-2}|k|^{1/2}\left(\frac{h}{p}\right)^{m^{\prime}+1/2},

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{A}}-\Pi_{p}^{\operatorname{curl},s}\mathbf{E}_{\mathcal{A}}\|_{\operatorname{imp},k}

\displaystyle\leq C|k|C_{\mathbf{j},\mathbf{g},\mathcal{A}}\left(\left(\frac{h}{h+\sigma}\right)^{p}+\left(\frac{|k|h}{\sigma p}\right)^{p}\right).

Numerical results

Figure 10.1:

\Omega=(-1,1)^{3}
, smooth solution; left to right:
p\in\{1,2,3\}

Figure 10.2:

\Omega=(-1,1)^{3}\setminus[-1/2,1/2]^{3}

p\in\{1,2,3\}

We illustrate the theoretical findings of Theorem
9.7

and Corollary
9.8
by two numerical experiments. All
computations are performed with NGSolve,
[
Sch
,
Sch97
]

using Nédélec type II elements, i.e., full polynomial spaces.

Remark 10.1

While the analysis of the present paper is peformed in detail for
Nédélec type I elements, it can be extended to
Nédélec type II elements. Key is the observation that
commuting diagram operators
\widehat{\Pi}^{grad,c}_{p+1}
and
\widehat{\Pi}^{curl,c}_{p}
analogous
to the ones used in Sections
8
,
9
for
type I elements also exist for type II elements. This is discussed in

[
Roj20
, Sec. 4.8]
.

We show in Figs.
10.1
,
10.2
the relative error in the
norm
\|\operatorname{curl}\cdot\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}+\left|k\right|\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}(\Omega)}\sim\|\cdot\|_{\mathbf{H}(\operatorname{curl},\Omega),\left|k\right|}

versus the number of degrees of freedom per wavelength

N_{\lambda}=\frac{2\pi\operatorname{DOF}^{1/3}}{|k||\Omega|^{1/3}},

where
\operatorname{DOF}
stands for the dimension of the ansatz space.

Example 10.2

We consider
\Omega=(-1,1)^{3}
and impose the right-hand
side and the impedance boundary conditions in such a way that the exact solution is

\mathbf{E}\left(\mathbf{x}\right)=\operatorname{curl}\sin(kx_{1})(1,1,1)^{\top}
. Fig.
10.1
shows the performance for the choices

k\in\{10,20,30,40\}
and
p\in\{1,2,3\}
as the mesh is refined
quasi-uniformly.
The final problem sizes were
\operatorname{DOF}=18,609,324
for
p=1
,

\operatorname{DOF}=9,017,452
for
p=2
, and
\operatorname{DOF}=23,052,940

for
p=3
.

We observe the expected asymptotic
O(h^{p})
convergence. We also observe
that the onset of asymptotic quasi-optimal convergence is reached for smaller
values of
N_{\lambda}
for higher order methods. This is expected in view of
Theorem
9.7
, although the present setting of a
piecewise analytic geometry is not covered by
Theorem
9.7
.

Example 10.3

We consider
\Omega=(-1,1)^{3}\setminus[-1/2,1/2]^{3}
and Maxwell’s equations with impedance boundary
conditions on
\partial(-1,1)^{3}
and perfectly conducting boundary
conditions on the inner boundary
\partial(-1/2,1/2)^{3}
. We prescribe an exact solution

\mathbf{E}(\mathbf{x})=k\cos(kx_{1})(x_{1}^{2}-1/4)(x_{2}^{2}-1/4)(x_{3}^{2}-1/4)(0,-1,1)^{\top}
. Fig.
10.2
shows the
performance for the choices
k\in\{20,40,80\}
and
p\in\{1,2,3\}
as the mesh
is refined quasi-uniformly.
The final problem sizes were
\operatorname{DOF}=43,598,374
for
p=1
,

\operatorname{DOF}=168,035,046
for
p=2
, and
\operatorname{DOF}=54,063,558
for
p=3
.

We observe the expected asymptotic
O(h^{p})
convergence. We also observe
that the onset of asymptotic quasi-optimal convergence is reached for smaller
values of
N_{\lambda}
for higher order methods.

Appendix A
Analytic regularity with bounds explicit in the wavenumber
(
[
NT20
,
Tom19
]
revisited)

In this appendix, we reproduce
[
NT20
, Appendix A]
and add
parts (marked in
red color
that are required to
account for the addition of right-hand sides in Theorem
5.2
. We
will also follow the numbering in
[
NT20
, Appendix A]
and
indicate insertions by “+1/2”,
“+1/3” etc.

To be consistent with
[
NT20
]
, we follow in this appendix the
“French” convention and denote

\mathbb{N}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\mathbb{N}^{\ast}=\{1,2,\ldots\}.

Furthermore, we assume as in
[
NT20
]
that
k\geq k_{0}>0
,
while the estimates for negative
k\leq-k_{0}<0
follow by replacing
k
by

\left|k\right|
in
all estimates.

For a bounded open set
\omega
, the seminorms
|\cdot|_{p,\omega}
are
defined by

|u|_{p,\omega}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\colon|\alpha|=p}\frac{|\alpha|!}{\alpha!}\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{2}(\omega)}^{2},\qquad\|u\|_{p,\omega}^{2}=\sum_{l=0}^{p}|u|_{l,\omega}^{2}.

(A.0)

We assume that
{\mathbf{f}}
is an analytic function satisfying

|{\mathbf{f}}|_{p,\Omega}\leq C_{\mathbf{f}}\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(
A.1
)

Additionally, given a tubular neighborhood
{\mathcal{T}}
of
\partial\Omega

and (analytic) functions
{\mathbf{G}}_{D}:{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{N_{1}}

{\mathbf{G}}_{B}:{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{N_{0}}
that satisfy

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}}_{D}\|_{p,{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}\qquad\forall p\in\mathbb{N},

(
A.1
+1/3)

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}}_{B}\|_{p,{\mathcal{T}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}\qquad\forall p\in\mathbb{N}.

(
A.1
+2/3)

We consider the two traces
{\mathbf{g}}_{D}:=\gamma\mathbf{G}_{D}
and

{\mathbf{g}}_{B}:=\gamma\mathbf{G}_{B}
. Then, we can formulate in
analogy to
[
NT20
, Thm. A.1]

Theorem A.1

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
n\leq 3
, be a bounded domain
with an analytic boundary
\partial\Omega
, and let
(L,D,B)
be an elliptic
system in the sense of

[
CDN10
, Def. 2.2.31, Def. 2.2.33]

with
L
(resp. D and B) an
N\times N
(resp.
N_{0}\times N
and
N_{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{1}}}\times N
with
N_{0}
,
N_{1}\in{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\mathbb{N}}}
such that
N_{0}+N_{1}=N
) system of differential operators of order
2

(resp.
0
and
1
) with analytic coefficients,
N\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
and

k>1
. Let
{\mathbf{f}}
,

{\mathbf{g}}_{D}
,
{\mathbf{g}}_{B}

be analytic functions verifying (
A
)

and (
+1/3
), (
+2/3
)

and
{\mathbf{G}}
a matrix with analytic coefficients

(in a tubular neighborhood of
\partial\Omega
)

. If
{\mathbf{u}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(\Omega)}}
is a solution of

\left\{\begin{array}[c]{ccll}L({\mathbf{u}})&=&{\mathbf{f}}+k^{2}{\mathbf{u}}&\text{ in $\Omega$},\\
D({\mathbf{u}})&=&{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathbf{g}{{}_{D}}}&\text{ on $\partial\Omega$},\\
B({\mathbf{u}})&=&k{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{+{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}}&\text{ on $\partial\Omega$,}\end{array}\right.

(
A.2
)

then we have

|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,\Omega}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p},\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}},p\geq 2

with
C_{\mathbf{u}}=\left({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{k^{-2}}}C_{\mathbf{f}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}+k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}}+}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\Omega}+k^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,\Omega}\right)
.

Remark A.1+1/3

The point of Theorem
A.1
is the tracking of the
k
-dependence. The key
ingredient of the proof below is the appeal to
[
CDN10
, Prop. 2.6.6]

and
[
CDN10
, Prop. 2.6.7]
, which in turn rely on

(a)

local
H^{2}
-estimates of the form
[
CDN10
, (2.47)]
. These local

H^{2}
-estimates result from the ellipticity of the operator
L
and the
covering condition of the boundary conditions (cf.
[
CDN10
, Cor. 2.2.16]
);

(b)

the condition
[
CDN10
, (2.50)]
. Condition
[
CDN10
, (2.50)]
is
satisfied by the ellipticity of the operator
L
(see footnote
3
in

[
CDN10
, p. 97]
).

Example A.1+2/3

We transform the problem

\displaystyle\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{E}}-k^{2}{\mathbf{E}}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{f}}\quad\mbox{ in $\Omega$},

(
A.2
+1/5)

\displaystyle\gamma_{T}\operatorname*{curl}{\mathbf{E}}-\operatorname{i}k{\mathbf{E}}_{T}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{g}}_{T}\quad\mbox{ on $\partial\Omega$},

(
A.2
+2/5)

for sufficiently smooth
{\mathbf{f}}
,
{\mathbf{g}}_{T}
to the form
considered in Theorem
A.1
. We proceed as in
[
NT20
]

and write the Maxwell equations as a first order system for the electric field

\mathbf{E}
and the magnetic field
\mathbf{H}:=-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}
and set
\mathbf{u}=\left(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{H}\right)
. The function
{\mathbf{u}}
solves the elliptic system

\begin{array}[c]{cclc}L\left(\mathbf{u}\right):=&\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}-\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{E}\\
\operatorname*{curl}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{H}-\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{H}\end{array}\right)&=\mathbf{F}+k^{2}\mathbf{u}&\text{in }\Omega,\\
D\left(\mathbf{u}\right):=&\mathbf{H}\times\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{E}_{T}&=-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\mathbf{g}_{T}&\text{on }\Gamma,\\
B\left(\mathbf{u}\right):=&\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{E}\\
\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{H}\\
\gamma_{T}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{H}+\left(\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}\right)_{T}\end{array}\right)&=kG\mathbf{u}+\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}&\text{on }\Gamma\end{array}

(
A.2
+3/5)

for

\mathbf{F}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\mathbf{f}+\frac{1}{k^{2}}\nabla\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}\\
-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right),\text{\hskip 10.22217pt}G\mathbf{u}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}0\\
0\\
\operatorname*{i}\left(\mathbf{H}_{T}-\gamma_{T}\mathbf{E}\right)\end{array}\right)\text{,\hskip 10.22217pt}\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\left.\left(\operatorname*{div}\mathbf{f}\right)\right|_{\Gamma}\\
0\\
-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\gamma_{T}\mathbf{f}\end{array}\right).

(
A.2
+4/5)

Elliptic systems of the form (
+3/5
) are considered in
[
NT20
, Thm. A1]
for
\mathbf{g}_{T}=\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}=\mathbf{0}
.

We now show that (
+3/5
) is indeed an elliptic system in the sense of

[
CDN10
, Def. 2.2.31]
. The operator
L
is simply the block Laplacian
since
\operatorname{curl}\operatorname{curl}=\nabla\operatorname{div}-\Delta

and clearly elliptic. To see that the boundary conditions are
covering

at a boundary point
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}
, we first consider the system
(
+3/5
) in a half space
\{x_{3}>0\}
with boundary
\{x_{3}=0\}
. We
assume that
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}=(0,0,0)^{\top}
. The principal part

L^{\mathsf{pr}}
of
L
coincides with
L
. In the notation of

[
CDN10
, Notation 2.2.1]
, the spaces
\mathfrak{M}[L;\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}]
and
\mathfrak{M}_{+}[L;\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}]
(with
\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
) can be computed explicitly due to the simple
structure of
L
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}[L;\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}]=\{{\mathbf{u}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}e^{-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|t}+\mbox{\boldmath$\beta$}e^{|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|t}\,|\,\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$},\mbox{\boldmath$\beta$}\in\mathbb{C}^{6}\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{+}[L;\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}]=\{{\mathbf{u}}(t)

\displaystyle=\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}e^{-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|t}\,|\,\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}\in\mathbb{C}^{6}\}.

In particular, the operator
L
is properly elliptic,
[
CDN10
, Def. 2.2.3]
. The boundary operators are obtained by inserting
\mathbf{u}
({\mathbf{x}}^{\prime},t)=e^{\operatorname{i}{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}}e^{-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|t}\mbox{\boldmath$\eta$}
into the boundary conditions and afterwards removing the factor

e^{\operatorname{i}{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime}\cdot\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}}
and setting
({\mathbf{x}}^{\prime},t)=(0,0,0)={\mathbf{x}}_{0}
,
which gives

\displaystyle D({\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\eta_{5}-\eta_{1}\\
-\eta_{4}-\eta_{2}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle B({\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{1}+\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\eta_{3}\\
\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{4}+\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{5}-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\eta_{6}\\
-\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{6}-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\eta_{4}+\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{3}+|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\eta_{2}\\
-\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{6}-|\xi^{\prime}|\eta_{5}-\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}\eta_{3}-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\eta_{1}\end{array}\right).

In matrix form, we have

\underbrace{\left(\begin{array}[c]{cccccc}-1&0&0&0&1&0\\
0&-1&0&-1&0&0\\
\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}&\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}&-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|&0&0&0\\
0&0&0&\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}&\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}&-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|\\
0&|\xi^{\prime}|&\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}&-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|&0&-\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}\\
-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|&0&-\operatorname{i}\xi_{1}^{\prime}&0&-|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|&-\operatorname{i}\xi_{2}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)}_{=:{\mathbf{M}}}\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}\eta_{1}\\
\eta_{2}\\
\eta_{3}\\
\eta_{4}\\
\eta_{5}\\
\eta_{6}\end{array}\right).

A direct calculation shows
\operatorname{det}{\mathbf{M}}=|\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}|^{4}
. Hence,
{\mathbf{M}}
is invertible for every
\mbox{\boldmath$\xi$}^{\prime}\neq 0
. This means that the set of boundary conditions satisfies the
covering condition. According to
[
CDN10
, Def. 2.2.31]
, this covering
condition has to be satisfied for every
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}\in\partial\Omega

and the “tangent” model system. Locally
flattening the boundary at
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}
in such a way that the Jacobian
is orthogonal at
{\mathbf{x}}_{0}
leads to “tangent” systems that are of the same form as above. Hence,
the system (
+3/5
) is elliptic.

While
[
NT20
, Cor. A.2]
focuses on the
regularity of
\mathbf{u}
, we formulate the following corollary differently
by giving a regularity assertion for for
\mathbf{E}
.

Corollary A.2

Let
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be a bounded domain with an
analytic boundary. Let the function
{\mathbf{f}}
satisfy (
A
).

Let the function
{\mathbf{g}}:{\mathcal{T}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{3}

satisfy for a tubular neighborhood
{\mathcal{T}}
of
\partial\Omega

|{\mathbf{g}}|_{p,{\mathcal{T}}}\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p},\quad\forall p\in\mathbb{N}.

Let
{\mathbf{E}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(\Omega,\operatorname*{curl})
solve
(
+1/5
), (
+2/5
). Then, we have

|{\mathbf{E}}|_{p,\Omega}\leq C_{\mathbf{E}}K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}},\quad p\geq 2,

where, for some
C
,
K>0
independent of
k
,

C_{\mathbf{E}}=C\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}k^{-2}+\left|k\right|^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}}+\frac{1}{\left|k\right|}\left\|\mathbf{E}\right\|_{\mathbf{H}^{1}\left(\Omega,\operatorname*{curl}\right),k}\right).

Proof.

We note that the analyticity of
\partial\Omega
implies that the normal
vector
\mathbf{n}
has an analytic extension
\mathbf{n}^{\star}
to a
tubular neighborhood of
\partial\Omega
. Since the operators
\gamma_{T}
,

\Pi_{T}
have the form
\gamma_{T}\mathbf{v}=\gamma(\mathbf{v}\times\mathbf{n}^{\star})
,
\Pi_{T}\mathbf{v}=\gamma(\mathbf{n}^{\star}\times(\mathbf{v}\times\mathbf{n}^{\star}))
, we see that
G\mathbf{u}
and

\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}
of (
+4/5
) can be written as

G\mathbf{u}=\gamma\widetilde{G}\mathbf{u},\qquad\mathbf{G}_{\Gamma}=\gamma\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}_{\Gamma},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}_{\Gamma}:=\left(\begin{array}[c]{c}-\frac{1}{k^{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\\
0\\
-\frac{\operatorname{i}}{k}\mathbf{f}\times\mathbf{n}^{\star}\end{array}\right),

where the matrix-valued function
\widetilde{G}
is analytic in a tubular
neighborhood of
\partial\Omega
. Likewise, the boundary data
\mathbf{g}_{T}=\gamma(\mathbf{n}^{\star}\times(\mathbf{g}\times\mathbf{n}^{\star}))=\gamma\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
for some
\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
that is
analytic in a tubular neighborhood of
\partial\Omega
. By, e.g.,

[
MS21b
, Lemma 2.6]
, we get by setting
\mathbf{G}_{B}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{G}}_{\Gamma}
,
\mathbf{G}_{D}:=-\operatorname{i}k^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}
for suitable
K_{\mathbf{f}}
,

\widetilde{\lambda}_{\mathbf{g}}
independent of
k

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{F}}\|_{p,\Omega}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{\mathbf{f}}K_{\mathbf{f}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p},\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}}_{B}\|_{p,\Omega}

\displaystyle\leq k^{-1}CC_{\mathbf{f}}K_{\mathbf{f}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p},\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{G}}_{D}\|_{p,\Omega}

\displaystyle\leq k^{-1}CC_{\mathbf{g}}\widetilde{\lambda}_{\mathbf{g}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p},\quad\forall p\in{\mathbb{N}}.

We may apply Theorem
A.1
with
G=\widetilde{G}
and
{\mathbf{F}}

given by (
+3/5
). To that end, we observe that the regularity
assumption
{\mathbf{E}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(\Omega,\operatorname*{curl})

implies
\mathbf{u}=\left(\mathbf{E},\mathbf{H}\right)=\left(\mathbf{E},-\frac{\operatorname*{i}}{k}\operatorname*{curl}\mathbf{E}\right)\in\mathbf{H}^{2}\left(\Omega\right)
so that Thm.
A.1
is indeed
applicable.

Remark A.3

See

A.1
Analytic regularity near the boundary

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0\leq\rho\leq R/(2p)}\rho^{p}|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,q,B_{R-p\rho}^{+}},\qquad\mbox{ for all $p>0$},

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0\leq\rho\leq R/(2(p+1))}\rho^{p+2}|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,q,B_{R-(p+1)\rho}^{+}},

\displaystyle|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n-1}\colon|\alpha^{\prime}|=p}\|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}},

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0\leq\rho\leq R/(2(p+1))}\rho^{p+\frac{3}{2}}|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R-(p+1)\rho}},

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}:=\max_{\alpha^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n-1}\colon|\alpha^{\prime}|=p}\|\partial^{\alpha^{\prime}}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}},}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}:=\max_{0\leq\rho\leq R/(2(p+1))}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}+p}|{\mathbf{u}}|_{p,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R-(p+1)\rho)}},}

for all
p
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
with
q\leq p
.

We will first estimate the norm of the tangential derivatives (and the normal
derivative up to 2) by using the standard analytic regularity of elliptic
systems. Then, we will be able to estimate the complete norm
{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}
. So we start with an estimation of the norm
of tangential derivatives
{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,2,B_{R}^{+}}
. Before,
let us prove the next technical results that allow to pass from a sum on the
multi-indices to a sum on their lengths.

Lemma A.4

Let
h
be a mapping from
\mathbb{N}
into
[0,\infty)
and
a multi-index
\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n-1}
, for
n=2
or
n=3
.
Then we have

\sum_{\beta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{n-1}\colon\beta^{\prime}\leq\alpha^{\prime}}h(|\beta^{\prime}|)\leq 2\sum_{p=0}^{|\alpha^{\prime}|}h(p)e^{|\alpha^{\prime}|-p}.

(A.6)

Proof.
See
[
NT20
, Lemma A.4]
.

Corollary A.5

Let
h
be a mapping from
\mathbb{N}
into
[0,\infty)
and a multi-index

\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
with
\alpha_{n}\leq 1
. Then we have

\sum_{\beta\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\colon\beta\leq\alpha}h(|\beta|)\leq 2(1+\frac{1}{e})\sum_{p=0}^{|\alpha|}h(p)e^{|\alpha|-p}.

(A.8)

Proof.
See
[
NT20
, Cor. A.5]
.

Lemma A.6

There exists a positive constant
C
(depending on
n
), a
positive constant
C_{tr,R}
(depending only on
R\leq 1
), and a positive
constant
\lambda_{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\prime}\geq\lambda_{{\mathbf{G}}}
such that
for all
l\in\mathbb{N}
and any
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{l+1}(B_{R}^{+})
, we have

\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\leq CC_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{G}}}\sum_{p=0}^{l+1}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\prime})^{l+1-p}\max(l+1,k)^{l+1-p}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,2,B_{R}^{+}}.

(A.9)

Additionally, the constant
C_{tr,R}
may be assumed to satisfy the trace
estimate

\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\leq C_{tr,R}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\qquad\forall{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{1}(B_{R}^{+}).

Proof.
See
[
NT20
, Lemma A.6]
.

Noting the trace estimate (cf.
[
NT20
, (A.10)]
), we obtain
from (
+1/3
), (
+2/3
) for the traces
{\mathbf{g}}_{D}
and

{\mathbf{g}}_{B}
for suitable
C_{tr,R}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{D}]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\left[{[\![{\mathbf{G}}_{D}]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}+{[\![{\mathbf{G}}_{D}]\!]}_{l+2,2,B_{R}^{+}}\right]

(
A.13
+1/4)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:A4a})}{\leq}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{l+2}\max(l+2,k)^{l+2},

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{B}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{tr,R}\left[{[\![{\mathbf{G}}_{B}]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}+{[\![{\mathbf{G}}_{B}]\!]}_{l+1,1,B_{R}^{+}}\right]

(
A.13
+1/2)

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:A4b})}{\leq}2C_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{l+1}\max(l+2,k)^{l+1},

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\|{\mathbf{v}}\|_{2,B_{R}^{+}}\qquad\forall{\mathbf{v}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(B_{R}^{+}).

(
A.13
+3/4)

Here, we have implicitly assumed
\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\geq 1
and

\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\geq 1
. We also emphasize that the constants

C_{tr,R}
and
C_{tr,R}^{\prime}
depend on
R
, which could be tracked by a
more careful application of the trace estimates.

Lemma A.7

Let
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(B_{R}^{+})
be a
solution of (
A.5
) with
{\mathbf{f}}
,

{\mathbf{g}}_{D}
,
{\mathbf{g}}_{B}
,

{\mathbf{G}}
analytic and satisfying (
A.3
)—(
+2/3
). Then
there exist
K>1
and
C_{R}>1
such that for all
p\geq 2

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}

with

C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})=C_{R}(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}+k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+k^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}})
.

Proof.
We will prove this result by induction, by applying a standard analytic
regularity result (i.e.,
[
CDN10
, Prop. 2.6.6]
), which gives us a real
number
A\geq 1
such that for all
p\geq 2

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\left(\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)+A^{p-1}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}.

(A.14)

Initialization:
For
p=2
, by (
A.14
) we have

\displaystyle{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{2,2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq A\left(\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{0,0,B_{R}^{+}}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{0,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{0,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)+A\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq A\left(\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![{\mathbf{f}}+k^{2}{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{0,0,B_{R}^{+}}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![k{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}]\!]}_{0,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{D}]\!]}_{0,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)+A\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\overset{R\leq 1}{\leq}A\left(\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+k^{2}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+k\|{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\|{\mathbf{g}}_{B}\|_{\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\|{\mathbf{g}}_{D}\|_{\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)+A\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\ref{eq:A13a}),(\ref{eq:A13b})}{\leq}A\left(\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+(k^{2}+1)\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+kC_{tr,R}\|{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+C_{tr,R}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{B}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{D}\|_{2,B_{R}^{+}}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\right)

with the constants
C_{tr,R}
,
C_{tr,R}^{\prime}

introduced before. By noticing that

\displaystyle kC_{tr,R}\|{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}CkC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}}\left(\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}+\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}\right),

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{B}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leq CC_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}k},

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\|{\mathbf{G}}_{D}\|_{2,B_{R}^{+}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leq CC_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}k^{2}}

we then have

\displaystyle{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{2,2,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq A\bigl(\|{\mathbf{f}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+(k^{2}+1+CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}k)\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+(CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}k+1)\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+A\bigl(CC_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}k\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}+CC_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}k^{2}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}\bigr)}

\displaystyle\leq Ak^{2}\Bigl[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+k^{-1}CC_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}+CC_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}+}

\displaystyle\qquad(2+CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}})\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+(CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}})k^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq Ak^{2}\max(2+CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}}\lambda_{\mathbf{G}},CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}CC_{tr,R}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}},CC_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}})\times

\displaystyle\qquad\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}+C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+k^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}C_{R}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})\max(2,k)^{2}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{2}\max(2,k)^{2},

with
C_{R}\geq A\max(2+CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}}\lambda_{\mathbf{G}},CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-1}},{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}CC_{tr,R}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}},CC_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}})\geq 1
and since
K\geq 1
.

Induction hypothesis:
For all
2\leq p^{\prime}\leq p
, we have

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p^{\prime},2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p^{\prime}}\max(p^{\prime},k)^{p^{\prime}}.

(A.15)

We will show this estimate for
p+1
: Using (
A.14
), we may write

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p+1,2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\left(\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)+A^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}.

(A.16)

We now need to estimate each term of this right-hand side. We start by
estimating
\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}
for

l\leq p-1
: First we notice that

\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}\overset{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}R\leq 1}}{\leq}{[\![{\mathbf{f}}+k^{2}{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}\leq{[\![{\mathbf{f}}]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}+k^{2}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,2,B_{R}^{+}}.

By the induction hypothesis (
A.15
), we then have

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{f}}\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{l}\max(l,k)^{l}+k^{2}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l}\max(l,k)^{l}

\displaystyle\leq k^{2}\max(l,k)^{l}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l}\left({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{l}}{K^{l}}}+1\right).

As
l+2\leq p+1
, this estimate directly implies that

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l}\left(\left(\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{l}}{K}\right)^{l}+1\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l},

for
K>\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
. Multiplying this estimate by
A^{p-l}
and
summing on
l
, one gets

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}\frac{2}{K}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}K^{l-p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}\frac{2}{K}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}\left(\frac{A}{K}\right)^{p-l}.

If
K\geq 2A
, then
\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}(\frac{A}{K})^{p-l}\leq\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}(\frac{A}{K})^{l}\leq 1
, which yields

\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,0,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}\frac{2}{K}.

(A.17)

Estimation of
\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}
:
By the boundary condition on

{\mathbf{u}}
, we have

\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\leq k\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{B}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}

and by the estimate (
A.9
)

and (
+1/2
)

, we get

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq kCC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}(\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime})^{l+1-p^{\prime}}\max(l+1,k)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p^{\prime},2,B_{R}^{+}}

(A.18)

\displaystyle\qquad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+2C_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{l+1}\max(l+2,k)^{l+1}=:I+II.}

For the first term,
I
,
the induction hypothesis (
A.15
) then leads
to

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I}

\displaystyle\leq kCC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}(\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime})^{l+1-p^{\prime}}K^{p^{\prime}}\max(l+1,k)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}\max(p^{\prime},k)^{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq kCC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})\max(l+1,k)^{l+1}K^{l+1}\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}}{K}\right)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}.

Hence, for
K\geq 2\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}

and
K\geq\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}

(recalling that
l+2\leq p+1
and that
\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}}{K}\right)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}\leq 2
), we
deduce

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}I+II}\leq 2CC_{tr}C_{\mathbf{G}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}

\displaystyle\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+2C_{tr,R}k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{l+1}\max(l+2,k)^{l+1\color[rgb]{1,0,0}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{+1)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{l+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}.

Multiplying this estimate by
A^{p-l}
and summing on
l
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{+1)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}K^{l-p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+1})\frac{A}{K}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{A}{K}\right)^{l}.

Again, for
K\geq 2A
, we arrive at

\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}\frac{4C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+1})A}{K}.

(
A.19
)

Estimation of
\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}
:

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle=\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{D}]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\overset{(\ref{eq:A13a})}{\leq}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{l+2}\max(l+2,k)^{l+2}

\displaystyle\overset{l+2\leq p+1}{\leq}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{l+2}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}.

Multiplying this estimate by
A^{p-l}
and summing on
l
, we get for

K\geq\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}
and
K\geq 2A

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{l}

\displaystyle\overset{\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\leq K}{\leq}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}K^{l}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}K^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p-1}A^{p-l}K^{l-p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}K^{p}\frac{A}{K}\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{A}{K}\right)^{l}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}K^{p+1}\frac{4C_{tr,R}^{\prime}A\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}}{K^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}K^{p+1}\frac{4C_{tr,R}^{\prime}A\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}}{K^{2}}.

(
A.19
+1/3)

Finally using the definition
C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})
, we directly check
that

\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}\leq\frac{k}{C_{R}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})

(
A.19
+2/3)

and therefore (since we assume that
K\geq 2A
)

A^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}\leq\frac{1}{C_{R}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}.

In summary, using this estimate (
A.17
), (
A.1
), and

(
+1/3
)
, in (
A.16
) we have obtained

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p+1,2,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p+1}\max(p+1,k)^{p+1}\left(\frac{2+4C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+1})A+\frac{1}{C_{R}}}{K}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\frac{4C_{tr,R}^{\prime}A\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}}{K^{2}}}\right).

This yields (
A.15
) for
p+1
if

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}K\geq\max\left(\lambda_{\mathbf{f}},\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}},2A,\lambda_{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\prime},2+4C_{tr,R}(CC_{\mathbf{G}}+1)A+\frac{1}{C_{R}}+4C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{2}A\right).}

Now, we will show an equivalent lemma but which also estimates the norm of the
normal derivatives of higher order.

Lemma A.8

Let
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(B_{R}^{+})
be a
solution of (
A.5
) with
{\mathbf{f}}
,
{\mathbf{G}}
,

{\mathbf{G}}_{D}
, and
{\mathbf{G}}_{B}

analytic and satisfying (
A.3
), (
A.1
), (
+1/3
),
(
+2/3
). Then there exist
K_{1}
,
K_{2}\geq 1
such that for all

,

with
q\leq p
, we have

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q}\max(p,k)^{p},

with
C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})=C_{R}\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}+k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}+C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R}^{+}}+k^{-1}\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{1,B_{R}^{+}}\right)
.

Proof.
Again, we will show this lemma by induction and by using a standard analytic
regularity result for elliptic problems (i.e.,
[
CDN10
, Prop. 2.6.7]
),
which gives us

\displaystyle{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\left\{\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-2)}B^{q-1-\nu}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}+B^{q-1}\left(\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad+A^{p-1}B^{q-1}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}

(A.21)

for some positive constants
A
and
B\geq 1
. The induction is done on
q
,
the initialization step
q=2
is obtained from Lemma
A.7
by taking

K_{1}\geq K
and
K_{2}\geq 1
. The induction hypothesis is: For all
p\geq 3
,

2\leq q^{\prime}\leq p-1
, it holds

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q^{\prime},B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q^{\prime}}\max(p,k)^{p}.

(A.22)

We use the estimate (
A.1
) to get

\displaystyle{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,q+1,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\left\{\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-1)}B^{q-\nu}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}+B^{q}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}B^{q}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}\right\}

\displaystyle+A^{p-1}B^{q}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}.

(A.23)

Estimate of
\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}
. By the induction hypothesis (
A.22
) we may write

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq{[\![{\mathbf{f}}]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}+k^{2}{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{f}}\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}^{l}\max(l,k)^{l}+k^{2}C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{l}K_{2}^{\nu}\max(l,k)^{l}

\displaystyle\leq C_{{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{l}K_{2}^{\nu}k^{2}\max(l,k)^{l}\left(\left(\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}}{K_{1}}\right)^{l}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{K_{2}^{\nu}}}+1\right)

\displaystyle\overset{l\leq p-2}{\leq}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}\frac{2}{K_{1}K_{2}}K_{1}^{l-p+1}K_{2}^{\nu-q}

if
K_{1}\geq\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}
. Multiplying this estimate by

A^{p-1-l}B^{q-\nu}
and summing on
\nu
and
l
, one gets

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-1)}B^{q-\nu}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}\frac{2}{K_{1}K_{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-1)}B^{q-\nu}K_{1}^{l-p+1}K_{2}^{\nu-q}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}\frac{2}{K_{1}K_{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}\left(\frac{A}{K_{1}}\right)^{p-1-l}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-1)}\left(\frac{B}{K_{2}}\right)^{q-\nu}.

Choosing
K_{1}\geq 2A
and
K_{2}\geq 2B
, we conclude that

\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}\sum_{\nu=0}^{\min(l,q-1)}B^{q-\nu}\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![L({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\nu,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}\frac{8}{K_{1}K_{2}}.

(A.24)

Estimation of
\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}
for
l\leq p-2
:
We use the estimate
(
A.9
) and the induction hypothesis (
A.22
)
and (
+1/2
)
to get

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leq\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![k{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}+\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![{\mathbf{g}}_{B}]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}}

\displaystyle\leq kCC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{2}^{2}\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}(\lambda_{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\prime})^{l+1-p^{\prime}}K_{1}^{p^{\prime}}\max(l+1,k)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}\max(p^{\prime},k)^{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+2C_{tr,R}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{l+1}\max(l+2,k)^{l+1}.}

In the above right-hand side as
l+2\leq p
and
p^{\prime}\leq p-1
, we
obtain

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq CC_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{G}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{2}^{2}\max(p,k)^{p}K_{1}^{l+1}\sum_{p^{\prime}=0}^{l+1}\left(\frac{\lambda_{\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}}{K_{1}}\right)^{l+1-p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+2C_{tr,R}k^{-1}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{B}}^{l+1}\max(p,k)^{p}.}

For

and

we deduce that

\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}\leq 2C_{tr,R}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}(CC_{{\mathbf{G}}}K_{2}^{2}+1)}\max(p,k)^{p}K_{1}^{l+1}.

(
A.25
)

Multiplying this estimate by

and summing on

, as before

and

)

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}B^{q}\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{3}{2}}{[\![B({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{1}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}B^{q}\max(p,k)^{p}2C_{tr,R}(CC_{{\mathbf{G}}}K_{2}^{2}+1)\sum_{l=0}^{p-2}A^{p-1-l}K_{1}^{l+1}K_{1}^{-p}}

\displaystyle\overset{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}K_{2}\geq B}}{\leq}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\left(\frac{4C_{tr,R}A(CC_{\mathbf{G}}K_{2}^{2}+1)}{K_{1}K_{2}}\right).}

Estimation of
\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}
: From (
+1/4
) and
l+2\leq p
we have

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{\frac{1}{2}}{[\![D({\mathbf{u}})]\!]}_{l,\frac{3}{2},\Gamma_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}C_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{l+2}\max(l+2,k)^{l+2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}.

(
A.25
+1/2)

Finally, using (
+2/3
), one has

A^{p-1}B^{q}\sum_{l=0}^{1}{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{l,l,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}\frac{1}{C_{R}K_{1}K_{2}}.

Inserting this estimate and the estimates (

),

)

), we can conclude that

\displaystyle{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{p,q+1,B_{R}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\max(p,k)^{p}}\left(\frac{8}{K_{1}K_{2}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{4C_{tr,R}A(CC_{\mathbf{G}}K_{2}^{2}+1)}{K_{1}K_{2}}}+\frac{1}{C_{R}K_{1}K_{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{2C_{tr,R}^{\prime}}{K_{2}^{q+1}}\left(\frac{\lambda_{{\mathbf{g}}_{D}}}{K_{1}}\right)^{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K_{1}^{p}K_{2}^{q+1}\max(p,k)^{p}

for
K_{1}
and

large enough.

Remark A.9

In Lemma
A.8
, if we take
p=q
, we obtain

{[\![{\mathbf{u}}]\!]}_{p,p,B_{R}^{+}}\leq C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}

with
K=K_{1}K_{2}
.

A.2
Interior analytic regularity

Let
B_{R}=B(0,R)
,
L
an elliptic system of order
2
defined in
B_{R}
,
and
k>1
. Here, we consider a solution
{\mathbf{u}}
of

\displaystyle L({\mathbf{u}})={\mathbf{f}}+k^{2}{\mathbf{u}}\quad\mbox{ in $B_{R}$}.

(A.26)

We now define the following semi-norms

\displaystyle{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{p,B_{R}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0<\rho<\frac{R}{2p}}\max_{|\alpha|=p}\rho^{p}\|\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R-p\rho}},

\displaystyle\rho_{\ast}^{2}{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{p,B_{R}}

\displaystyle:=\max_{0<\rho<\frac{R}{2p}}\max_{|\alpha|=p}\rho^{p+2}\|\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbf{u}}\|_{B_{R-p\rho}}.

We suppose that
{\mathbf{f}}
is analytic with

\displaystyle\|\partial^{\alpha}{\mathbf{f}}\|_{B_{R}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathbf{f}}\lambda^{p}_{\mathbf{f}}\max(|\alpha|,k)^{|\alpha|},\quad\forall\alpha\in{\mathbb{N}}^{n},

(A.27)

for some positive constants
C_{\mathbf{f}}
and
\lambda_{\mathbf{f}}

independent of
k
.

Lemma A.10

Let
{\mathbf{u}}\in{\mathbf{H}}^{2}(B_{R})
be a solution
of (
A.26
) with
{\mathbf{f}}
satisfying (
A.27
). Then there
exists
K\geq 1
such that

{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{p,B_{R}}\leq C{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}

with
C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{R}^{+})=C_{R}(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{B_{R}}+k^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{1,B_{R}})
for

suitable

C_{R}\geq 1
.

Proof.
The proof is exactly the same as the one of Lemma
A.7
when we use

[
CDN10
, Prop. 1.6.3]
(a standard interior regularity result) instead
of
[
CDN10
, Prop. 2.6.6]
.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
A.1

According to a standard procedure, see for instance
[
CDN10
, p. 105]
,
the first step of the proof is to consider a covering of
\Omega
by some open
sets, which verifies

\displaystyle\Omega\subset\cup_{j=1}^{N}\hat{B}_{j}\subset\cup_{j=1}^{N}B_{j},

where

1.

B_{j}=B(\mathbf{x}_{j},\xi_{j})
and
\hat{B}_{j}=B(\mathbf{x}_{j},\frac{\xi_{j}}{2})
, with
\xi_{j}>0
small enough such that

\overline{B}(\mathbf{x}_{j},\xi_{j})\subset\Omega
, if
\mathbf{x}_{j}\in\Omega
,

2.

while in the case
\mathbf{x}_{j}\in\partial\Omega
,
B_{j}
is a
sufficiently small neighborhood of
\mathbf{x}_{j}
such that there exists an
analytic map
\phi_{j}
from
B_{j}
onto the ball
B(0,\xi_{j})
(for some

\xi_{j}>0
such that
\phi_{j}(B_{j}\cap\Omega)=B_{j}^{+}
, and
\phi_{j}(B_{j}\cap\partial\Omega)=\Gamma_{\xi_{j}}
. In that case, we set
\hat{B}_{j}=\phi_{j}^{-1}(B_{\xi_{j}/2}^{+})
.

This yields

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\Omega}\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{N}|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{j}\cap\Omega}\lesssim\sum_{1\leq i\leq N\colon\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\Omega}|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{j}}+\sum_{1\leq i\leq N\colon\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\partial\Omega}|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{j}\cap\Omega}.

In the case of an interior ball, namely, for
i
such that
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\Omega
, we simply perform a translation to apply Lemma
A.10
.
Hence, the operator
L
does not change, and we directly
have
11
11

11

We employ the multinomial formula and the definition
of the norm
|\cdot|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}}
given in (
A.0
) so that the
formula deviates from
[
NT20
]

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}}\lesssim{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{p/2}}{[\![\mathbf{u}]\!]}_{p,{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}p},B_{i}}\lesssim{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{p/2}}C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{i})K^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}.

By the definition of
C_{\mathbf{u}}(B_{i})
, we then arrive at

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}}\lesssim\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{B_{i}}+k^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{1,B_{i}}\right)({\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{1/2}}K)^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}.

(A.28)

In the case when the open set intersects the boundary of
\Omega
, namely, for
each
i
such that
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\partial\Omega
, we apply the change of
variables
\mathbf{\hat{x}}=\phi_{i}(\mathbf{x})
, which allows us to pass
from
B_{i}\cap\Omega
to
B_{\xi_{i}}^{+}
and transforms the system
(
A.2
) restricted to
B_{i}\cap\Omega
into an elliptic system with
analytic coefficients of the form (
A.5
) with other operators
\hat{L}
,
\hat{D}
,
\hat{B}
, which
\hat{D}
(resp.
\hat{B}
) an operator of
order
0
(resp.
1
). First thanks to a Faà-di-Bruno formula, we obtain
(see
[
CDN10
, (1.b)]
)

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}\cap\Omega}\lesssim c_{i}^{p+1}\sum_{l=0}^{p}\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}p!}}{l!}|\hat{\mathbf{u}}|_{l,B_{\xi_{i}/2}^{+}},

with a positive constant
c_{i}
which depends only on the transformation that
allows us to pass from
B_{i}\cap\Omega
to
B_{\xi_{i}}^{+}
. Then, we can
apply Lemma
A.8
(see Remark
A.9
) and get

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}\cap\Omega}\lesssim{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{p/2}}c_{i}^{p+1}C_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{\xi_{i}}^{+})\sum_{l=0}^{p}\frac{{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}p!}}{l!}K^{l}\max(l,k)^{l}.

Using
[
NT20
, (A.11)]
,

i.e.,

\frac{p!}{q!}\leq p^{p-q},\quad\forall p,q,\in{\mathbb{N}}\colon q\leq p,

and a change of variables (in
C_{\hat{\mathbf{u}}}(B_{\xi_{i}}^{+})
and
again Faà-di-Bruno formula) we obtain

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}\cap\Omega}\lesssim{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{p/2}}c_{i}^{p+1}\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{B_{i}\cap\Omega}+k^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{1,B_{i}\cap\Omega}\right)\max(p,k)^{p}\sum_{l=0}^{p}K^{l}.

This yields

|\mathbf{u}|_{p,\hat{B}_{i}\cap\Omega}\lesssim\frac{c_{i}K}{K-1}\left(C_{\mathbf{f}}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}k^{-2}}+\|\mathbf{u}\|_{B_{i}\cap\Omega}+k^{-1}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{1,B_{i}\cap\Omega}\right)(c_{i}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}n^{1/2}}K)^{p}\max(p,k)^{p}.

The combination of this estimate with (
A.28
) yields the result.

Appendix B
Details of the proof of
Lemma
8.3

In this section, we give some details of the proof of
Lemma
8.3
. The estimates of the
volume terms
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K})}
,
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}
in
(
8.10
) are proved in

[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
and it remains to estimate the
boundary norms in (
8.10
). For this,
we first reproduce the arguments given in
[
MS10
, Appendix B]
to make our proof for the boundary norms
(Theorem
B.5
) self-contained.

Before proceeding we recall the definition of the Sobolev space
H_{00}^{1/2}(\Omega)
. If
\Omega
is an edge or a face of a triangle or a
tetrahedron, then the Sobolev norm
\|\cdot\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(\Omega)}
is
defined by

\|u\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(\Omega)}^{2}:=\|u\|_{H^{1/2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\left\|\frac{u}{\sqrt{\operatorname*{dist}(\cdot,\partial\Omega)}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2},

(B.1)

and the space
H_{00}^{1/2}(\Omega)
is the completion of
C_{0}^{\infty}(\Omega)
under this norm.

Definition B.1
(element-by-element construction,
[
MS10
, Def. 5.3]
)

Let
\widehat{K}
be the reference simplex in

{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
,
d\in\{2,3\}
. A polynomial
\pi
is said to permit

an element-by-element
construction of polynomial degree
p
for
u\in H^{s}(\widehat{K})
,
s>d/2
, if:

(i)

\pi(V)=u(V)
for all
d+1
vertices
V
of

\widehat{K},

(ii)

for every edge
e
of
\widehat{K}
, the restriction

\pi|_{e}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
is the unique minimizer
of

\pi\mapsto p^{1/2}\|u-\pi\|_{L^{2}(e)}+\|u-\pi\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}

(B.2)

under the constraint that
\pi
satisfies (
i
); here the
Sobolev norm
H_{00}^{1/2}
is defined in (
B.1
).

(iii)

(for
d=3
) for every face
f
of
\widehat{K}
, the
restriction
\pi|_{f}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
is the unique minimizer of

\pi\mapsto p\|u-\pi\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|u-\pi\|_{H^{1}(f)}

(B.3)

under the constraint that
\pi
satisfies (
i
),
(
ii
) for all vertices and edges of the face
f
.

Lemma B.2
(
[
MS10
, Lemma B.1]
)

Let

\widehat{K}^{2D}
be the reference triangle in 2D. Vertex and edge lifting
operators can be constructed with the following properties:

1.

For each vertex
V
of
\widehat{K}^{2D}

there exists a polynomial
L_{V,p}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
that attains the value

1
at the vertex
V
and vanishes on the edge of
\widehat{K}^{2D}
opposite
to
V
. Additionally, for every
s\geq 0
, there exists
C_{s}>0
such that

\|L_{V,p}\|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K}^{2D})}\leq C_{s}p^{-1+s}
.

2.

For every edge
e
of
\widehat{K}^{2D}

there exists a bounded linear operator
\pi_{e}:H^{1/2}_{00}(e)\rightarrow H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{2D})
with the following properties:

(a)

\forall u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}\cap H_{00}^{1/2}(e):\quad\pi_{e}u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p},

(b)

\forall u\in H_{00}^{1/2}\left(e\right):\quad\pi_{e}u|_{\partial\widehat{K}^{2D}\setminus e}=0,

(c)

\forall u\in H_{00}^{1/2}\left(e\right):\quad p\|\pi_{e}u\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K}^{2D})}+\|\pi_{e}u\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{2D})}\leq C\left(\|u\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}+p^{1/2}\|u\|_{L^{2}(e)}\right)
.

Lemma B.3
(
[
MS10
, Lemma B.2]
)

Let

\widehat{K}^{3D}
be the reference tetrahedron in 3D. Vertex, edge, and face
lifting operators can be constructed with the following properties:

(i)

For each vertex
V
of
\widehat{K}^{3D}

there exists a polynomial
L_{V,p}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
that attains the
value
1
at the vertex
V
and vanishes on the face opposite
V
.
Additionally, for every
s\geq 0
there exists
C_{s}>0
such that

\|L_{V,p}\|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K}^{3D})}\leq C_{s}p^{-3/2+s}
.

(ii)

For every edge
e
of
\widehat{K}^{3D}
there
exists a bounded linear operator
\pi_{e}:H^{1/2}_{00}(e)\rightarrow H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{3D})
with the following properties:

(a)

\pi_{e}u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
if
u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}\cap H^{1/2}_{00}(e)

(b)

(\pi_{e}u)|_{f}=0
for the two faces
f
with
\overline{f}\cap e=\emptyset

(c)

for the two faces
f
adjacent to
e
(i.e.,
\overline{f}\cap e=e
)

\displaystyle p\|\pi_{e}u\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|\pi_{e}u\|_{H^{1}(f)}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}+p^{1/2}\|u\|_{L^{2}(e)},

\displaystyle\|\pi_{e}u\|_{H^{1/2}(\partial\widehat{K}^{3D})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(p^{-1/2}\|u\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}+\|u\|_{L^{2}(e)}\right),

\displaystyle p\|\pi_{e}u\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K}^{3D})}+\|\pi_{e}u\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{3D})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(p^{-1/2}\|u\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}+\|u\|_{L^{2}(e)}\right).

(iii)

For every face
f
of
\widehat{K}^{3D}
there
exists a bounded linear operator
\pi_{f}:H^{1/2}_{00}(f)\rightarrow H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{3D})
with the following properties:

(a)

\pi_{f}u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
if
u\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}\cap H^{1/2}_{00}(f)

(b)

(\pi_{e}u)|_{f^{\prime}}=0
for the faces
f^{\prime}\neq f

\displaystyle p\|\pi_{f}u\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K}^{3D})}+\|\pi_{f}u\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K}^{3D})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|u\|_{H^{1/2}_{00}(f)}+p^{1/2}\|u\|_{L^{2}(f)}\right).

Lemma B.4
(
[
MS10
, Lemma B.3]
)

Let
\widehat{K}
be the
reference triangle or the reference tetrahedron. Let
s>d/2
. Then there
exists for every
p
a bounded linear operator
\pi_{p}:H^{s}(\widehat{K})\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
and for each
t\in[0,s]
a constant
C>0

(depending only on
s
and
t
) such that

\|u-\pi_{p}u\|_{H^{t}(\widehat{K})}\leq Cp^{-(s-t)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},\qquad p\geq s-1.

(B.4)

Additionally, we have
\|u-\pi_{p}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}\leq Cp^{-(s-d/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
. For the case
d=2
we furthermore have

\|u-\pi_{p}u\|_{H^{t}(e)}\leq Cp^{-(s-1/2-t)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}

for
0\leq t\leq s-1/2
for every edge. For the case
d=3
we have
\|u-\pi_{p}u\|_{H^{t}(f)}\leq Cp^{-(s-1/2-t)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
for

0\leq t\leq s-1/2
for every face
f
and
\|u-\pi_{p}u\|_{H^{t}(e)}\leq Cp^{-(s-1-t)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
for
0\leq t\leq s-1
for
every edge.

The following theorem is a slight modification of
[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
and expresses the statement of
Lemma
8.3
.

Theorem B.5
(Lemma
8.3
/
[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
)

Let
\widehat{K}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
be the reference tetrahedron. Let
s>3/2
. Then there
exists
C>0
(depending only on
s
) and for every
p
a linear operator

\pi:H^{s}(\widehat{K})\rightarrow{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
that permits an
element-by-element construction in the sense of
Definition
B.1
such that

\displaystyle p\|u-\pi u\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K})}+\|u-\pi u\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-1)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}\qquad\forall p\geq s-1,

(B.5)

\displaystyle p^{1/2}\|u-\pi u\|_{L^{2}(\partial\widehat{K})}+p^{-1/2}\|u-\pi u\|_{H^{1}(\partial\widehat{K})}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-1)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}\qquad\forall p\geq s-1.

(B.6)

Proof.
We also mention that
(
B.5
) is shown in

[
MS10
, Thm. B.4]
so that our reproducing the proof
focuses on ensuring that
(
B.6
) holds.

We will construct
\pi u
for a given
u
; inspection of the construction
shows that
u\mapsto\pi u
is in fact a linear operator.

Let
\pi^{1}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
be given by
Lemma
B.4
. Then, for
p\geq s-1
there
holds

\displaystyle\|u-\pi^{1}\|_{H^{t}(\widehat{K})}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-t)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},\qquad 0\leq t\leq s

(B.7)

\displaystyle\|u-\pi^{1}\|_{H^{t}(f)}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-t-1/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},\qquad\forall\mbox{ faces $f$},\quad 0\leq t\leq s-1/2

(B.8)

\displaystyle\|u-\pi^{1}\|_{H^{t}(e)}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-t-1)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},\qquad\forall\mbox{ edges $e$},\quad 0\leq t\leq s-1

(B.9)

\displaystyle\|u-\pi^{1}\|_{L^{\infty}(\widehat{K})}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}.

(B.10)

From (
B.10
) and the vertex-lifting properties given in
Lemma
B.3
, we may adjust
\pi^{1}
by vertex liftings to
obtain a polynomial
\pi^{2}
satisfying (
B.7
)–(
B.10
) and
additionally the condition (
i
) of
Definition
B.1
. We next adjust the edge values. The
polynomial
\pi^{2}
coincides with
u
in the vertices and satisfies
(
B.9
). By fixing a
t\in(1/2,s-1)
, we get from an interpolation
inequality:

\displaystyle p^{1/2}\|u-\pi^{2}\|_{L^{2}(e)}+\|u-\pi^{2}\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}

\displaystyle\leq p^{1/2}\|u-\pi^{2}\|_{L^{2}(e)}+C\|u-\pi^{2}\|_{L^{2}(e)}^{1-1/(2t)}\|u-\pi^{2}\|_{H^{t}(e)}^{1/(2t)}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}.

Hence, for an edge
e
, the minimizer
\pi^{e}
of the functional
(
B.2
) satisfies
p^{1/2}\|u-\pi^{e}\|_{L^{2}(e)}+\|u-\pi^{e}\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
; the triangle inequality therefore gives that the correction

\pi^{e}-\pi^{2}
needed to obtain condition (
ii
) of
Def.
B.1
likewise satisfies
p^{1/2}\|\pi^{e}-\pi^{2}\|_{L^{2}(e)}+\|\pi_{e}-\pi^{2}\|_{H_{00}^{1/2}(e)}\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
. We conclude that the edge lifting of
Lemma
B.3
allows us to adjust
\pi^{2}
to get a polynomial

\pi^{3}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
that satisfies the conditions (
i
) and (
ii
) of Def.
B.1
. Additionally, we
have

\displaystyle p\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{L^{2}(\widehat{K})}+\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{H^{1}(\widehat{K})}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-1)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},

\displaystyle p\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{H^{1}(f)}

\displaystyle\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}\qquad\mbox{ for all faces $f$}.

Since
\pi^{3}|_{e}=\pi^{e}
for the edges, the minimizer
\pi^{f}
of the
functional (
B.3
) for each face
f
has to satisfy
p\|u-\pi^{f}\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|u-\pi^{f}\|_{H^{1}(f)}\leq p\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|u-\pi^{3}\|_{H^{1}(f)}\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})}
. From the triangle inequality, we conclude

p\|\pi^{3}-\pi^{f}\|_{L^{2}(f)}+\|\pi^{3}-\pi^{f}\|_{H^{1}(f)}\leq Cp^{-(s-3/2)}|u|_{H^{s}(\widehat{K})},\quad\mbox{ together with }\pi^{3}-\pi^{f}\in H_{0}^{1}(f).

Hence, the face lifting of Lemma
B.3
allows us to correct
the face values to achieve also condition (
iii
) of
Definition
B.1
. Lemma
B.3
also
implies that the correction is such that
(
B.5
) is true.
